# DESIGN_FP_ARCH_PR — THE PATROLS: a settlement's riders on its own roads, as small units of its own fighting strength

## Compiled on the owner's eight words of 2026-10-18 in the FP final architecture program (kit `settlement-engine-kits/fp-final-a73343ed-2026-10-17/`); DARK behind two virtual flags (`patrolsEnabled`, `patrolEffectsEnabled`, §12); nothing scheduled.
## CONSOLIDATED 2026-10-23 from the bracketed text of record at `1beabdc85` — the review trail is in git and in `settlement-engine-kits/fp-final-a73343ed-2026-10-17/history/PR-cure-record.md`; the compile log, every measurement (CONFIRMED by reading the cited symbol at master `81f1cd963`) and the open questions: `settlement-engine-kits/fp-final-a73343ed-2026-10-17/cure/L-PR.md` (probes `cure/L-PR-probe/`).
## Bound by the FP constitution's requirements 1–24 (`DESIGN_FP_SPINE.md` §1; 15–24's text of record is `DESIGN_FP_COHERENCE_FOLD.md` §1) and by the fold. ONE persisted shape (`spatialLedgers.patrols`, four keyed maps) plus ONE conditional stamp on the landed deployment record (§2.7); the owner's shapes and rows are marked ⭐; every ruling is vetoable (§17); every constant is DRAFT and UNMEASURED on the FP-46 roster.

---

## THE READER'S GUIDE

1. WHAT THIS IS. A settlement's riders on its own roads: a small detachment of the town's own garrison and levy, placed once a week on one place and never stepped (PR1), counted inside an envelope four existing reads set (§3), walking a round of road halves by address and week (PR4, §2.3), reacting, grouping, dispersing and hunting on what the town believes (§5), and leaning — once each, bounded, at one named seam — on quantities the world already keeps (§6). It adds a weekly placement digest and one keyed ledger, never a second danger, gate row, head count or strength number (PR3, PR5).
2. ITS ONE OWNER. This volume owns THE ONE PATROL STAGE (`src/domain/patrols/patrolStage.js :: advancePatrols`, §4.1) and its ledger `spatialLedgers.patrols` (§2.7); the ONE encounter law `patrolEncounter` (§8.1); the witness read `witnessedCrossing` (PS8, PR10); the ONE believed-watch read every court planner calls (§8.3); `patrolDraw` (§6.6); the riders' law of THE BANNER (§10.3); the realm verb `PATROL_ORDER` (§11.1); the hearing kinds `patrol_engages` and `patrol_joins` (§11.2); and the closed words of §2.
3. WHAT IT READS. BELIEF for the courts' decisions — the one threat read and the holder's own belief record of a den (§5.1), the believed-watch read (§8.3), the stance read (`beliefMap.js :: belief`, §8.1), fame at the riders' town (`fameIn`, §8.1), the hunt's prediction and the known ways (§5.5), the sightings a look writes (§9.2 (b)), the crown's hold as each decider believes it (THE BANNER, §10.3 (d)); TRUTH where the world acts — the week's placement every consumer reads (PR4), the encounter's MET and its catch (PR7, §8.1), the witness (PR10, §6.5), the gleaning roll's sign on the ground (§5.5), the envelope's four gates, each its own truth (tier, census, purse, order — PR3, §3.1), the capacity model (PR3, §6.6), THE SHARED PRESENCE COMPOSER, which resolves the physics (§0.2), and the deciding court's own facts — its war front and its army's deployment, its posture and alignment (PR2, §3.2, §9.1 (e)); and every belief read returns the truth where beliefs are dark and inside the town's land under the omniscient arm (§5.1, §10.1 (a), §12).
4. WHAT IT WRITES, one writer each: the week's placement and the ledger's maps by THE ONE PATROL STAGE; a report as a rumor feed entry with the town its hop-0 witness, through the rumor road's one writer (PR7); the conditional deployment stamp by one line of the opener (§2.7, §6.6); each lean through its host's one seam (§6).
5. WHERE IT MOUNTS. A leaf third at the head of `calamityKernel.js :: advanceCalamity` (after MN's and ST's stages); the screen in PS's `TRANSIT_LEAVES` list; the draw in `warDeployment.js :: evaluateWarLayer`; the stamp's normalizer in `worldState.js :: normalizeDeployments`; the fast half at `embattlement.js :: banditryLoss`; the smuggling watch at `smuggle.js :: smuggleSuccessChance`; the counter term through ST's one post-blend slot; zero lines in `pulseKernel.js`, `applyWorldPulse.js`, `warTermination.js`, `roadsKernel.js` and `armyTransitKernel.js` (PR16).
6. FLAGS: `patrolsEnabled` · `patrolEffectsEnabled` (§12). HEARING KINDS: `patrol_engages` · `patrol_joins` (§11.2). CHAIN: C16 THE PATROLS in the fold's §6, after C15 THE SITES (C14 · C15 · C16 share the border gate), waves PR-0 to PR-5 (§14).
7. DEPENDS ON: MN (THE SHARED PRESENCE COMPOSER; the counter term's one road to a menace's wear), ST (the post-blend slot, the funding read, a held keep's rows), PS (the ladder, the hold helper, `armsNear`, the jurisdiction read), PL (the banner stack, the one share, the hold), BD (the bodies, the ways, the cover), ES (the spy's catch), PT (`party_gate`, `party_ambush`), CH, WY, RN, the rumor road and the belief map. READ BY: PS (`witnessedCrossing`), ES and every court planner (the believed-watch read), BD and CH (the coverage of a known way), MN (the slow half's feeder row), the host's window (§13).
8. THE WORDS NOT TO CONFUSE: the prose "round" (the riders' weekly walk; the state `on_round`) versus the estate's NEWS BEAT `beat`; the crown's riders (`command: 'paramount'`) versus the landed `warden` role, EC's `route_wardenship` and the map's "wardens' terms"; the map's cadence words versus MN's might words, `RESERVE_BANDS` and PS's tolerance band; "passage" — THE PASSAGE's crossing (PS) — versus a charter's toll right, `toll_exemption` (PL §4.5), which is never leave to cross.

**The dispatch gate is the owner's.** Nothing here dispatches a build: push, merge, deploy, migration and dispatch stay the owner's. The rulings in §17 are by delegation under the owner's words, vetoable by one clause; the owner's own rows are marked ⭐.

## THE ANSWER IN ONE PARAGRAPH (for the DM)

Every town big enough to keep a watch puts a few riders on its own roads. How many, and how good, is set by four things the world already knows: the town's size (a hamlet keeps one thin patrol at most, a city many), its people (the same men the levy would call), its wealth (what it can pay them), and how seriously it keeps order. War and fear move the number inside those limits: more riders out when an enemy is believed near or the town is at war, fewer in quiet years; when the town's army goes on campaign its riders keep the army's road out first, and the back roads go to the brigands unless the town keeps riders there too — men it then does not send with the army. Each week the riders walk a round of the town's roads, the most dangerous and the richest first; where they walk, bandits strike less often, smugglers are caught more often, spies and enemy captains risk being stopped, and the town hears what passes on its land. When the town believes something is out there, its riders go and look; when the thing is bigger than one patrol, several ride together; when it is gone, or was never there, they go back to their rounds — so a false report costs a town its watch for a while. When war comes, the riders are the first to see it and the first to die buying the town a week. Allied towns' riders pass each other at the shared gates and trade what they saw. Inside a kingdom every patrol rides under its own town's banner and every banner above it to the crown's: where the crown's hold is strong the kingdom's riders are one net across the members' borders — a town whose riders went to the war is covered by the crown's and its neighbours' — where the hold is weak each lord keeps his own roads, and where a lord rebels the crown's riders are strangers at his border. The riders also HUNT what keeps off the roads — the gangs, the outlawed flocks and the beasts — by what their town believes of where the thing went and the hidden paths it knows, and they leave alone the flocks and bands their court lets be. Nothing the riders do is a second law: each effect is a bounded lean inside a number the world already keeps. The DM sees the deeds ("Kell's riders came in from the north road with word of Merewick's host at the ford"); the reasons wait for a look on purpose; the DM can direct the riders, hold their pay down (or raise the town's purse to raise it), and decide every clash.

### 0.2 The binding prior words
- **The fold's ruling on patrol coverage** (fold §5 row `patrol`; OD-FC-2): "patrol coverage's host meaning is a bounded counter term on the raider row's counterforce, behind a flag … one danger truth, never a lowering of danger by drawing (the map's own law)"; and "FP mints no `patrol` token", "its producer WC's raiding arm". THIS VOLUME, on the owner's word, mints `patrol` and becomes the producer; the fold row's "FP mints no `patrol` token" and its "producer WC's raiding arm" are superseded by the owner's 06:0x word, and the counter term it ruled is kept exactly (PR5).
- **MN §5.5** `menaceCounterTerms`: slot (2) is "FILLED THROUGH THE STAMP, never added here" — PR's counter term enters ST's ONE post-blend slot and reaches a menace's wear only through the stamped `counterforce.score` (`stressors.js :: ageRoamingStressors`); PR adds no row to MN's list (§6.2).
- **ST §3.4 / ST8 / ST item 21, MN §3.3** — ONE presence term of FOUR registered feeders (MN §3.3: "`p(T) = max(wallsTerm(T), columnTerm(T), max(0, roadCountry(T) − σ(T)))`" — the walls, an enemy column, the road-country; OD-MN-40: "THE BODIES' row `bodyPresenceFeeder` registers in the one composer's road-country part … — FOUR feeders": a menace, an untied site, an enemy column and a body, the last three's road-country rows taking their MAX before σ), computed by THE SHARED PRESENCE COMPOSER, an OWNED unit — MN's, one manifest row, reading TRUTH (MN §3.3: "the composer is an OWNED unit — one manifest row, its owner volume THIS one … cut with whichever of C14 · C15 · C16 · C10 is cut first, never re-implemented by a caller — and it RESOLVES THE PHYSICS, so it reads TRUTH"), ONE suppressor slot of two suppressors (the held keep's and the riders') under ONE cap ("`σ(T) = min(0.25, siteSuppression(T) + patrolSubtraction(T))` … ONE suppressor slot, one shared cap"), on the road-country part only; ST §6.1 step 6 "a grouped patrol (THE PATROLS) … the other clearers"; ST17 the watchtower's witnesses; ST §7.4's coverage row for PR as it now stands — "BY BOTH — with THE PATROLS lit the town's riders walk a held keep's stretches first (PR §2.2), drawn from the town's ONE manpower pool (PR3); the keep's garrison is the SITE's own complement, drawn from no town's pool and never the riders, so the keep's road rows stand beside the riders' as two DISTINCT feeders of one capped σ (`min(0.25, σ_site + σ_patrol)`) and one counterforce composition — different men, never a double count; the levy draws riders, never a keep" — so the keep's road rows (presence suppression 0.20, raider counterforce +0.15, the three gate rows) STAND while it is held and funded, lit or dark, and its garrison is paid through the holder by the site's funding band (§10.5).
- **THE BANNER** (OD-PR-26) — the owner's ~19:2x word made a design: one home, a stack of banners, one share law, the line blurring by the crown's hold, the crown's protection felt and recorded, civil war closed, its counterforces and its surface (§10.3 carries it; PR11 states it).
- **THE BODIES (`DESIGN_FP_ARCH_BD.md`) and its routes to PR (`cure/L-BD.md` §4; BD §19's PR row)** — a body (a gang, a conclave, a beast) is a traveller the ONE encounter law meets (§8.1), a foe of `patrol_engages` (§11.2), the quarry of THE HUNT (§5.5), and a watcher of the same law when two bodies meet (BD §9.1); BD's words bind here: "a patrol's coverage on an UNKNOWN way is ZERO; on a known WATCHED way it is the round's coverage at the junctions (PR's one coverage read)" (BD §5.2), "Patrols pursue only what their court marks `hunted` or `outlawed` (and every beast); a `permitted` conclave is NEVER pursued; `curbed` is watched, not hunted" (BD §7.1). And the hidden ways ARE master's hidden rung — "ONE NETWORK: MASTER'S HIDDEN RUNG IS THE WAYS … BD's derived cover graph is ONLY the CANDIDATE geometry … PR (`cover01` from `hiddenWayEdges`) RE-POINT[S] [its] trail seams to the landed rung — one seam, one price" (BD §5.2: `routeNetworkLedger.js :: ROUTE_GRADES` "`['highway', 'road', 'track', 'hidden']`", `routeNetworkConsumers.js :: HIDDEN_PATH_KINDS` "`['covert_envoy', 'smuggler', 'wanderer']`" behind `mayUseHiddenPaths`, which "fails CLOSED", `routeNetworkConsumersTransit.js :: gradeLegMultiplier` with "`hidden: 2,`" pinned equal to `npcConsequencesTuning.js`'s "`HIDDEN_PATH_SLOWDOWN: 2,`" — CONFIRMED at master); ONE trail-knowledge read — "`knownTrailsOf(town)` = the stored learned set ∪ the warden OFFICE's derived ways (BD §2.3) — PS's `hiddenWayFor`, PR's `trailWatchOf` and CH's routing all call it; the office knowledge belongs to the person HOLDING the public-security office with the warden archetype (L-BD 41), never to any retained warden"; ONE HUNT, ONE DEED (§7.2, §9.3); THE NET never lends zero at a strong hold (§10.3 (c)); THE BANNER's cover a season ledger (⭐ OD-PR-50, §2.7, §10.3 (f)); the swept lord's grudge a resentment patch (§7.6, §10.3 (h)).
- **PS8** — "The noticing read is THE PATROLS' (`witnessedCrossing(worldState, holderId, gateKey, unit, tick)`, owned by `DESIGN_FP_ARCH_PR.md`) … Until THE PATROLS lands, the read answers `true`"; PR OWNS `witnessedCrossing` (OD-PR-13).
- **The host document** (HR:497–585): "Patrols by address and week, never a roll"; "Cadence never reaches the host as a time"; "No invented effects. A patrol is never drawn lowering danger or holding a quarantine line"; "Patrols are per road stretch; water has none … no sea patrol exists in the design, and the only water 'watch' is the host's own naval blockade (W11)"; a cadence is "weekly, every four weeks, every thirteen weeks, or never"; H16 "The only door through which patrols or the sway can ever touch the simulation" — the map's INBOUND door (the map's coverage coming into the host), which PR never opens (§13).
- **The quarantine verb** (plague-R7): a quarantine verb, when built, is "the host's own verb with a STANCE input … never a patrol fact" — patrols may ENFORCE a quarantine the host's verb sets (§9.2), never stand for it.
- **The coverage line** — each existing volume carries ONE "patrols considered" line where PR touches it (§15's coverage table is that list).

### 0.3 The sea and the seasons are already in the books — and in the code (the owner's question, answered with receipts)
- **Sea lanes:** `distanceRead.js :: pathCost` — "SEA LANES (M8) … a sea lane embarks at a port"; the landed digest slot `reserved.seaLanes`; TR S10 and EC S35 record M8's capacity.
- **Storms:** the same function — "Land hops weather the seasonal terrain law; sea hops the storm law (pathSeasonMultiplier)"; `roads/seaRoads.js` (D-6, `seaRoadsEnabled`): "storm delay (S2, priced not severed)".
- **Piracy:** `roads/seaRoads.js` — "piracy (S3 = banditry-by-construction)", "the T3 twin VERBATIM (× embattlement level)" — the owner's "piracy at bandit parity", landed.
- **Navies, convoys and sea battles (W-NAVY, owner-commissioned):** `docs/DESIGN_NAVY.md` — "navies convoy own/allied armies on water routes; hostile navies meeting = a sea battle with LAND-PARITY semantics (loser retreats to home port)"; "One navy per port settlement"; `navalKernel.js` — "SEA BATTLES: two HOSTILE navies whose paths share a SEA EDGE collide there", "BLOCKADE … MINTS A SIEGE"; WY §3 "`armyTransit` + `navalTransit` (same record shape, `mode:'sea'`)"; blockade capture S1 needs `navalEnabled`.
- **Seasons and weather:** SEASONS-B (M3) "a frozen `reserved.seasonalOverlay` … winter reshapes route CHOICE (a mountain pass balloons near-impassable; the longer plains detour wins)"; `seasons.js :: seasonalSeverityFor` (`drought · hard_winter · bountiful`) on the food year; `climateBandFor` (W-CAP CAP-3, `harsh · standard · mild`) read only by the food year's amplitude; EC's conditions `harvest_short · harvest_rich · famine` (EC M7–M9).
- **The one thing still owed**: travel's seasonal law keys on TERRAIN through the overlay, not on the measured climate band (H11's accepted climate-band re-version is the open piece). When it lands, winter SLOWS a pass — toward `spatialCost.js :: SLOW_NOT_SEVER_MAX` ("Every value FINITE and ≥ 1, and ≤ SLOW_NOT_SEVER_MAX"; `distanceRead.js`: "even a max-winter mountain hop is SLOW, never infinite") — and NEVER closes it (the host document's law 15: "Weather slows travel, never cuts it"). PR inherits that bound through `hopWeeks` and pins it (R26).

### 0.4 Population facts that bound the design
- Territories are the host's seat partition (`spatialDigest.js`: every land cell to its cheapest-reached settlement; the gate between two neighbours "the cheapest crossing"); a settlement has a handful of neighbours, so a town's ROAD HALVES — the half on its own land of `road{seat, n}` for each digest neighbour `n` (§2.2) — number single digits; a whole world's stretch-weeks are bounded (tens of settlements × single-digit stretches).
- Patrols are FEW by the tier ceiling (§3.1): at most twelve per metropolis, one per hamlet, none per thorp.
- No patrol exists anywhere today (`patrol` as a code token: 0 files outside generation prose — `cure/L-PR-probe/tokens.out`).

### 0.5 What the measurement changed
Fourteen refinements of the brief, each in force where it sits (`cure/L-PR.md` §2): (R-1) a patrol is a weekly-placed detachment, never a column or a free unit — accepted as written (OD-PR-2); (R-2) the count is an integer and a stretch's coverage is the map's own cadence words — the brief's `none · thin · fair · strong` collides with MN's might words, `RESERVE_BANDS` and PS's tolerance band; (R-3) `beat` is the estate's NEWS BEAT — the state is `on_round`, the prose "round"; (R-4) contraband interception is `smuggle.js`, not the roads gauntlet (missions only); (R-5) PR mints NO stressor gate row — the landed rows already carry the standing watch; (R-6) the counter term is the DEVIATION from the tier's reference watch, never a second count of the institutions; (R-7) the fast half mounts at `banditryLoss` (goods); named travellers' T3 is frozen — ruled: the slow half alone for missions and sea piracy now, the fast half for missions a roads leaf-first car on C7; (R-8) the crown's patrols are `command: 'paramount'`, "the crown's riders" in prose — `warden` is a landed role, EC's `route_wardenship` and the map's "wardens' terms"; (R-9) PL's charter grants tolls only (`toll_exemption`, PL §4.5) — members' riders cross by PS's ladder; (R-10) `party_stopped` is registered nowhere — ruled: `party_gate` widened to a `road` WHERE; (R-11) the screen of an army marching on the patrol's OWN town is PR's hearing (PS1: the destination's land is never a passage), an incursion through a third town's land is PS's card; (R-12) fatigue is derived (no stock, no second decay law); (R-13) the crown's summons is not PR's: the brief's answer words `sent · kept` (PL's relief words; `answered` is a landed literal) yield to PL's `call_in_answer`, which is the crown's call on a member's riders; (R-14) no sea round at landing (the map: "water has none"; the sea is M8's, W-NAVY's and seaRoads') — the port's piracy falls by construction through the presence term.

---

## §1 THE LAWS (PR1–PR19)

**PR1 — A PATROL IS A DETACHMENT OF ITS SETTLEMENT'S OWN FIGHTING STRENGTH, PLACED WEEKLY, NEVER STEPPED.** A patrol is a small body of the town's garrison and levy that stands, each week, on exactly one PLACE: a road HALF of its round (the half on the town's own land of a road to a neighbour, cut at the digest gate — §2.2), a site, a looked-at place, or home at rest. ONE PLACE PER RIDER PER WEEK is a law the round's allocation keeps by construction (§2.3: a STANDING rider walks one half every week; a ROVING rider walks one half of its cycle a week, or is home), so the town's present halves in any week never outnumber its riders (EXECUTED `cure/D-PR-r3-probe/arith.out` (3): the allocation never places a rider twice in 208 weeks over every n 0..12 × H 1..9). It has no mid-route position and no stepper; a move of more than one hop takes the weeks the light-unit physics prices (`namedPersonLegTicks({ nominalWeeks: hopWeeks(digest, a, b, season) })`), during which it covers nothing. Its identity is positional — `(settlementId, ordinal)` within that week's count — and an ordinary patrol has no record of its own: the week's PLACEMENT is written once per tick as a digest every consumer reads (PR4, §2.7), and what a patrol DOES across weeks (a detachment's errand, a loss, the DM's order) is a row. Macro over micro: the table needs to know where the riders were and what they did, never their marching order. A patrol is neither a member of WC's column union nor a free unit: a `migration.js` column "carries no position" (PS §5.4) and is released at arrival, and WC-11's free unit (`freeUnits.js`) is ownerless and unbuilt; WC's people ledger (WC-6, unbuilt) is the slot where a patrol's men become a block when headcounts are conserved (OD-PR-2).

**PR2 — THE COUNT AND THE SIZE LIVE INSIDE AN ENVELOPE FOUR GATES SET; WAR AND BELIEF MOVE THEM ONLY INSIDE IT; THE COUNT IS NEVER AUTHORED.** TIER sets the CEILING, the town's PEOPLE (the people ledger's census count against its tier's reference population) set the POOL, prosperity (and the treasury when lit) sets the PAY, internal security sets the WILL (§3.1, the owner's eighth word). Inside that envelope the war state and the town's belief of what is out there set how many ride this week: fewer in peace (as many as the court's posture keeps out), all of them when the town is at war at home or an enemy is believed near (all it KEPT, while its own army is out), and with the town's army ON CAMPAIGN never fewer than in peace and MORE wherever the envelope has room — the army's road out watched first, the back roads left unless the court keeps riders there too (§3.2). The DM moves the inputs (wages, the town's order, war) and directs the riders (`PATROL_ORDER`, §11); the DM never sets a count.

**PR3 — ONE HEAD COUNT, ONE STRENGTH, ONE DRAW AT DEPLOY.** The riders are men of the town: the POOL reads the people ledger's census count (`settlement.population` — `peopleLedger.js :: POOLS` 'census'), the one head count; their fighting worth is part of the town's ONE strength truth, the capacity model (`buildCapacityLookup`'s `offensive`), from which the field army is SEEDED once, at deploy (`seedDeploymentState`'s `start`). So men on the roads are men not in the field: the riders the court KEEPS on its roads while its army campaigns are subtracted from the army's seed AT DEPLOY — `patrolDraw`, the chooser's trade-off at the moment the army is raised (§6.6), taken BEFORE the opener's target loop so the feasibility gates that choose the target judge the army that will actually march — and re-read only at a re-deploy; the per-tick `offensive` and `homeDefense` are never touched, and `resolveSiegeVerdict`'s stateful `currentEffectiveStrength` is untouched (riders at home are home: the walls do not lose men who are home). Whatever the town does not keep on its roads goes with the army (ABSORBED), and the roads it stops watching go unpatrolled; a court that keeps more riders keeps its rear safe and marches a smaller army — that is the decision (§9.1(e)). No second head count and no second strength number exist.

**PR4 — BY ADDRESS AND WEEK, NEVER A ROLL; ONE PLACEMENT A WEEK.** Whether a town's riders walk a road half in a given week is a PERIODIC CLOCK: the half's cadence sets the PERIOD (`weekly` 1 · `every_four_weeks` 4 · `every_thirteen_weeks` 13 · `never`) and keyed hashes of the half and the power — never of the week — set the PHASE (a roving half's slot in its rider's cycle, ordered by `kernel/proseHash.js :: fnv1a32` of the half and the power, plus that rover's own base offset, §2.3) (the host document's own law: "presence in a week is fixed by a hash of the stretch and the power, so every run agrees"; the map's own phase is likewise fixed "never from the week", HR:813), so `every_four_weeks` means exactly one week in four — the map's meaning of the word. The stage writes the coming week's PLACEMENT once (`spatialLedgers.patrols.placement`, §2.7), and EVERY consumer in that week — the banditry roll, the smuggling roll, the counter term, the slow half, the witness, the encounter, the report, the map's window — reads that one placement (a pin: they agree). Every other draw (notice, contest, look) is a seeded keyed hash with a named stream. Two runs of one world agree on every patrol in every week.

**PR5 — ONE DANGER, ONE COUNTERFORCE, AND NO GATE ROW.** Patrols change ONLY these quantities the world already keeps, each by ONE bounded lean at ONE named seam (§6, §7): (a) a SUBTRACTION on the ONE presence term of the danger ledger, inside the one suppressor slot of MN §3.3's composition — THE SHARED PRESENCE COMPOSER, a shared leaf-first car (OD-PR-43) — (ST's sites and PR's riders, one shared cap), floored at zero, never on the walls term — so a patrol lowers only what raiding menaces and untied dens ADDED to the country's roads — never the walls term and never an enemy column (MN §3.3) — and "peacetime roads carry no danger to relieve" (the host document) holds by construction; (b) a bounded DEVIATION term on the raider row's counterforce, and a coverage term on the criminal corridor's, riding ST's ONE additive post-blend slot under its ONE clamp — reaching a raiding menace's wear only through the stamped counterforce score (MN §5.5 (2)); (c) the banditry ROLL for goods on a watched hop (PR6); (d) the smuggling success chance at the worst gate (§6.4); (e) the field army's SEED at deploy, by the riders kept (`patrolDraw`, PR3) — never the per-tick `offensive` or `homeDefense`; (f) the commons' ONE grievance home, by ONE registered input (over-patrolling, §7.5); (g) the WITNESS read PS consumes (PR10); (h) PR's own WILL gate, by a magistrate's lean (§10.5). The hearings and receipts of §9–§11 decide between parties and change nothing else. PR NEVER mints a stressor birth gate row: the landed rows "Standing defenses patrol the approaches." and "A practised watch chokes smuggling at the gates." already ARE the standing watch, keyed on the institution roster; a second row would count the same riders twice. PR never touches the ramp's own security relief (`rampThreat`'s `W_SEC × security01`, the watch inside the walls).

**PR6 — THE FAST HALF LOWERS THE ROLL, PER HOP, NEVER THE LEVEL OR THE LOSS.** On the week a patrol (or detachment) actually stands on a road half, the INCIDENT ROLL for goods crossing THAT HOP is lowered: a per-hop multiplier on the hop's danger BEFORE the route's mean, feeding only `banditryLoss`'s fire test (§6.3); the loss SIZE still reads the unwatched mean, a sea hop reads no watch (no naval damper), and the embattlement level — the region's state — is never written by a patrol's presence (item 17: two mechanisms, one truth each, never doubled; their JOINT effect is one composition with one floor, §6.3). Named travellers' rolls live in the frozen roads kernel and feel patrols only through the slow half — ruled (§17): the slow half alone for missions and sea piracy now; the fast half for missions is a roads leaf-first car recorded as a C7 slot.

**PR7 — BELIEF NEVER TRUTH, EXCEPT WHERE THE WORLD ACTS.** A town's riders go where the town BELIEVES the trouble is and report what they SAW: a report is a rumor feed entry with the town as its hop-0 witness (the witness idiom), so it is the town's belief and only travels as rumor travels. Courts PLAN on belief — a traveller's court routes around the roads it believes watched (§8.3). The ENCOUNTER itself is the world acting on the traveller and is decided on truth (the patrols that actually walk the stretch), exactly as the roads gauntlet and ES's catch roll are ("the K3 boundary runs between a court's DECISIONS (belief) and the world's OUTCOMES (truth)", ES §3.3).

**PR8 — ONE ENCOUNTER LAW; WHAT A CATCH IS BELONGS TO EACH KIND'S OWNER.** `patrolEncounter` decides whether a traveller on a stretch MET a patrol (truth, PR4) and whether it was NOTICED (a keyed draw against coverage × quality × the traveller's cover). What happens next is never PR's second law: a spy's road catch is ONE roll — PR's notice IS that roll — whose outcome is ES's (`spy_at_target` with WHERE `road`: `caught · uncaught`, the taint cascade); an envoy is STOPPED only as a road hearing, THE ENVOY ARM of PS's `passage_at_the_border` (`stopped · escorted · turned_back`, PS §6.5), never the army interception's `envoy_intercepted`/`envoy_held`; a hostile paragon through CH's town's-gate words at WHERE `road`; the party through PT's `party_gate` widened to WHERE `road` (ruled, §17); goods through `smuggle.js`'s pipeline; a caravan through WY-6's `caravan_seized`; a military unit (an army, a column, a corps) through PS's witness and ladder; a BODY of THE BODIES (a gang, a conclave, a beast) through `patrol_engages` where its court stance is pursued, a report where it is only watched, nothing where it is let be (§8.1, §8.2, BD §9.3). Each power whose riders stand on the half meets the traveller separately — one encounter per power (§8.1). A traveller ON A HIDDEN WAY — a body, a paragon on CH's trail arm, a smuggler's run, a covert envoy, any mover master's hidden franchise admits — is met ONLY by the trail arm, on the way's junction halves of a power that knows the way (`trailWatchOf`, §8.1). The same law has a second WATCHER: when two bodies meet, MET and NOTICED are this law's and the card is BD's `bodies_meet` (§8.1).

**PR9 — REACT, GROUP, DISPERSE, BY BELIEF AND BY EFFECT.** A believed threat pulls riders toward it; a threat believed bigger than one patrol's band draws several into ONE detachment of the summed band; when the threat is gone, beaten, or was never there, the detachment disperses and its riders go back to their rounds. Grouping and dispersal are internal acts (receipts, no hearing). A FALSE REPORT therefore costs a town its watch for as long as it takes to look and find nothing — the effect of belief, priced by the world itself. Off their round the riders also HUNT (§5.5): a body their court has marked `hunted` or `outlawed`, or a beast, is pursued at the place the town BELIEVES it went — its last sighting, the trails the town knows, its habits — until it is caught or the trail goes cold; the round they leave goes unridden meanwhile, and a rider outmatched shadows and calls for others, never engages.

**PR10 — THE WITNESS IS THE PATROLS'.** `witnessedCrossing` (PS8) is owned here: a crossing is witnessed when a patrol of the HOLDER walks the holder's half of the gate's road that week (the placement, PR4), or, under a STRONG banner, a rider of the kingdom's net stands on the holder's half (§10.3 (d): the kingdom's eyes are one) (the owner's "operate in unity to cover the entirety of the kingdom region": a net rider placed on a member's half by right sees for that member, while the GATE's catch stays the holder's own riders', J385, §6.4), or the crossing's PLACE is the holder's own seat (a harbour landing or a circle arrival — never a land crossing whose path merely runs on through the seat), or the unit is too large to hide, or a held watchtower stands on the stretch (ST17). On a HIDDEN WAY (a corridor on master's hidden rung, keyed by THE BODIES' `trail:<a|b>`; PS8's quiet crossing taken off the road) the place is the WAY and ONE arm applies: witnessed only where the holder KNOWS the way (THE BODIES' one known set `knownTrailsOf`) and the holder's EYES — its own riders, or a net rider standing on its half by right (`holderEyesOf`, §2.6) — stand on one of the way's JUNCTION halves that week (`trailWatchOf`, §2.6; BD §5.2: "a patrol's coverage on an UNKNOWN way is ZERO") — the stretch-rider, seat, size and watchtower arms do not apply to a unit off the road (the way's capacity is the hiding law, PS8). A town with no riders is blind to its roads; the quiet crossing succeeds only where no patrol walks.

**PR11 — FRIENDS BY BELIEF; ONE HOME AND A STACK OF BANNERS; THE LINE BETWEEN MEMBERS BLURS BY THE CROWN'S HOLD.** Allied patrols know each other as far as their towns' beliefs go, and a patrol crossing an ALLY's or a stranger's border is a military unit crossing: PS's ladder applies, with a wider tolerance for a patrol's small size (§10.1(c)). Inside a KINGDOM every patrol has ONE home — the town that raises, pays and houses it, which has FIRST CALL on its own roads — and a LOYALTY STACK, the home's vassal chain up to the crown as PL reads it, derived each week and never stored (§10.3 (a)). The crown's share of a member's riders is its ONE share of the member's force and decisions (PL §4.7 F.2: "PL4's `POLITY_LIEGE_SHARE[band]` of the member's seat weight toward its IMMEDIATE liege, at every tier — never a second share for riders"), and under a strong hold a member LENDS at least one rider while it keeps the home's minimum (§10.3 (c)). Where the crown's OWN hold reads STRONG (`staunch`, `unbroken`: `polityHoldOf(…, rootId, rootId)`, PL §3.1 (e)) the kingdom is ONE NET: a member's riders ride a fellow member's roads BY RIGHT OF THE BANNER wherever that member believes the hold strong (PS's jurisdiction read — a `banner` permit, no spend, one card defaulting `granted`) — and the crown's riders and the members' lent riders fill the halves a member leaves unwatched where a need stands, under the per-stretch cap; where it is WEAK (`shaky`, `frayed`) each lord keeps his own roads and a neighbour-lord's riders — the crown's included — cross on the ladder with the ally's size tolerance; across a BROKEN rung (a feud, a break, a contest that suspends the link) recognition is lost and the crown's riders are a foreign unit under PS's rules. Members never cross by the charter, whose grant is tolls only (`toll_exemption`, PL §4.5). The crown's presence wears as PL's instruments wear when it sweeps a member's roads unasked (§7.6).

**PR12 — FIRST TO SEE, FIRST TO DIE.** When war comes, the riders' report is the town's first belief of it; a detachment may screen an invader on its town's land — whether the host marches on that town or crosses it toward another — for ONE bounded week, applied once at the screen's verdict by THE ONE HOLD HELPER's FREEZE mode (the unit held exactly where the prior tick left it, one week, no refund — PS §4.1) and, where THE PASSAGE is lit, counted into the march's ONE cumulative hold cap (PS9's Bound 2; dark, Bound 1 alone); PR hosts the screen's card and PS keeps the home-defence battle; then the riders the court does not keep fold into the levy (§9.1).

**PR13 — FIRST TO LOOK.** An appropriate stressor or an appropriate authored event in the territory sends ONE patrol to look for a week; the look is a belief read of what is there; the report suggests, never decides, for the DM (§9.2).

**PR14 — THE MAP DRAWS, NEVER INVENTS; H16 STAYS SHUT; THE HOST'S FACTS GO OUT THROUGH THE WINDOW.** While PR is dark the map's patrols are its own picture and its legend is true ("patrols change nothing in the simulation"). When PR lights, the host SENDS its patrol facts — each road half's power, cadence word and THE WEEK'S PRESENCE as it was (the placement, PR4), with state and detachment — out through the WINDOW, each on its own channel (the dated acts in the events window H9; the week just lived as a lens-window row H23 beside it; H13 carries the map's patrol inputs) — never "opens H16"; the host ASKS the map to draw presence as sent rather than recompute it — the map program's call, a cross-program ask, never a rule on the map — and the legend changes (§13). H16 — the map's INBOUND door — stays untouched and open as the owner left it; the host never consumes the map's sway-derived coverage. `patrolEffectsEnabled` lights only where the map's patrol lens is absent or has adopted the host's facts and legend (§12), so two patrol pictures and a false legend never stand together.

**PR15 — THE SEA AND THE SEASONS ARE ALREADY IN THE BOOKS; PR ADDS NEITHER.** Rounds are LAND road stretches at landing (the host document: "Patrols are per road stretch; water has none"). A port's piracy is S3, "banditry-by-construction" over the port node's embattlement level — so a town's riders, by lowering the presence term at its port node, lower piracy there with no line of their own. A patrol's move is priced by `hopWeeks(…, season)`, so winter slows it over a pass exactly as it slows a caravan. A harbour's watch is the port's own NAVY (W-NAVY: "One navy per port settlement"; sea battles between navies, blockade as a siege — `navalKernel.js`), never a PR round and never a new slot: PR mints no sea patrol and no sea law; the sea's carriers are M8 (lanes, ports, storm seasons), W-NAVY and seaRoads D-6 (S1–S3). Winter, when the climate band's travel consumer lands (H11's re-version), SLOWS a pass toward `SLOW_NOT_SEVER_MAX` and never closes it — PR inherits exactly that bound (R26).

**PR16 — THE MOUNT RULE.** Zero lines in `pulseKernel.js` (1581), `applyWorldPulse.js` (907), `warTermination.js` (818), `roadsKernel.js` (838) and `armyTransitKernel.js` (798) beyond PS's one shared stage call. THE ONE PATROL STAGE is a leaf third at the head of `calamityKernel.js :: advanceCalamity` (after MN's and ST's); the screen rides PS's `TRANSIT_LEAVES` registration list (third, PS §4.1's one order); the draw rides the opener in `warDeployment.js :: evaluateWarLayer` (685 eff, +2: the drawn capacity taken right after `fromCap`, read by both feasibility gates, the `CONQUEST_MARGIN` prefilter and the seed; and ONE conditional stamp line beside the seed, the landed `joinLedger` idiom — §6.6); `seedDeploymentState` (`warArmyRecord.js`, 72 eff) is NOT edited — the stamp is written by its own line in the opener; the stamp's fail-closed normalizer is ONE call in `worldState.js :: normalizeDeployments` (351 eff, +1, the `casusReasons`/`joinLedger` idiom; §16.1); PR makes NO edit to master's hidden-path access law — `routeNetworkConsumers.js :: TRAVELLER_KINDS` and `HIDDEN_PATH_KINDS` and their pins are untouched by PR, and the riders declare no hidden token (master's franchise rests on TWO pinned arrays: a token in `HIDDEN_PATH_KINDS` alone reds `tests/domain/routeNetworkConsumers.test.js` ("`for (const kind of HIDDEN_PATH_KINDS) expect(TRAVELLER_KINDS).toContain(kind);`")); every lean is a leaf its host calls in ≤ 3 lines; hosts near the ceiling (`stressorDynamics.js` 759) take their lines only through ST's one slot leaf.

**PR17 — THE DM'S HAND IS TOTAL.** `PATROL_ORDER` directs the riders — `first` on `round` puts every rider the envelope allows out, `avoid` on `round` holds the count at the minimum (§11.1) — and can hold their pay down (the DM's wages band is a CEILING over the purse's band, §3.1 — ST's one band law); every clash and join is a hearing the DM may leave, tilt or decide (requirement 24), and the crown's call on a member's riders is PL's `call_in_answer`, heard there; every row is undone by the advance's undo; the count is never authored (PR2). Surprise is safe because the DM can undo any advance, edit any state and decide any hearing (fold requirement 16).

**PR18 — BEHIND THE SCENES; THE EVIDENCE LAW.** The SURFACE shows the deed ("Kell's riders confiscated Varn cloth run past the embargo" — the landed branch an embargo gate takes is `confiscation`); SCRUTINY shows the stretch, the cadence word, the band, the order, the reasons; the MACHINERY (hashes, multipliers, caps) is never pushed. No sentence forecasts ("the riders will catch …"); a patrol's piece on the map says where it was this week, never where it is going (WY §3b: "A marker is a presence, never an intention"). Patrol lines are chronicle unless a clash, a catch of a named traveller, or a war's first report follows (the push budget).

**PR19 — PURPOSE IS THE DM'S; THE PARTY IS NEVER THE ENGINE'S TARGET.** Riders keep the roads; they never seek the party of their own accord — they ride for it only when a court's DM-visible `party_ambush` laid on a road (PT's kind, whose agent is "the WATCH") sends them: with PR lit that WATCH IS a detachment of the court's riders (aim `pursue`, drawn from the round, its cost borne on the round), and the hearing stays PT's; a `party_warrant` stands at the court's town and is served there, never on a road (PT's card). A stop of the party on the road is the world's ordinary act on a traveller — PT's `party_gate` at WHERE `road` — its default "nothing has happened to the party yet" (requirement 24's party clause). The world hosts the campaign and never competes with it.

---

## §2 THE ONTOLOGY

Every vocabulary below is closed; every token was searched on master (`cure/L-PR-probe/tokens.out`, `tokens2.out`, `literals.out`) and against the fold §5; homonyms are registered in §15.2.

### 2.1 The patrol (derived each week; never stored)
`patrolsOf(worldState, snapshot, settlementId, tick) → Patrol[]`, codepoint-stable, each `{ settlementId, ordinal, command, band, state, place, causedBy }` — READ by the DOSSIER's defence page (the town's riders, their places and their states, behind its toggle; the dossier is a reader):
- `command ∈ PATROL_COMMANDS = own · paramount` — the town's own riders, or the crown's riders (§10.3) quartered from the polity's root (PL's class word `paramount`, PL §2); a member's rider LENT to the banner's net keeps `own` — its home's — on a fellow member's half (the half's holder differs, which IS the banner; no third command word). The brief's "road wardens" is not minted: `warden` is a landed role label (`roleCategory.js`), CH's display noun and EC's `route_wardenship` / the map's "wardens' terms" — "the crown's riders" in prose, `paramount` in code.
- `band ∈` MN's might words `thin · modest · capable · strong · formidable` — the `STRENGTH_BANDS` keys MN mints (the J-CNC-5 keys; floors 78/60/42/26/0 on the 0..100 latent scale) — ONE strength scale for a patrol, a detachment, a menace and a body (THE BODIES' might reads the same keys, BD §2.6), so every contest compares like with like. The army's own landed strength phrases (`ARMY_STRENGTH_PHRASES`) are a display ladder; the five keys MN mints are the comparable scale.
- `state ∈ PATROL_STATES = on_round · converging · engaged · escorting · looking · resting` (§2.4).
- `place` — the ONE WHERE convention (fold §3.4 companion table): `{ kind: 'road', ids: [a, b], half: holderId }` (a road HALF — the road is `road{a, b}`, unordered, keyed `stretch:<a|b>` with the digest's own pair key; the half is the part on `holderId`'s land, cut at the digest gate, §2.2), `{ kind: 'settlement', ids: [seat] }` (home, resting), `{ kind: 'site', id }` (ST), `{ kind: 'country', ids: [a] }` (a look in the land around a town).

### 2.2 The round
**THE ONE STRETCH KEY**. A ROAD is unordered, `road{a, b}`, keyed `stretch:<a|b>` — the digest's own codepoint-sorted pair key (`spatial/distanceRead.js :: pairKey`: "The codepoint-stable unordered pair key (matches the digest's routeReceipts keys)", "`` a < b ? `${a}|${b}` : `${b}|${a}` ``"; the form `roads/seaRoads.js :: classifyLegModes` uses too); a power's HALF of it is the part on its own land, cut at the digest gate between the two territories (`spatialDigest.js`: the gate between two neighbours, "the cheapest crossing"), named by the pair `{ road: stretchKey, holder }` — never a third key form (`roadKey` everywhere in this volume IS this stretch key); `gateKey(prev, B) = road{prev, B}` — the gate is that road's cut point, and B's half is the one a unit entering B's land walks. A named traveller's half is chosen from its LEG's own position (`worldPulse/namedPersonTransit.js :: namedPersonPathPosition`: the leg's `progress01` is LEG-wide — "`nodeIndex = … Math.floor(alongStoredPath * last)`" — and `hopIndex` follows from it): it stands on the origin's half of hop `hopIndex` while `progress01 < (hopIndex + cut01) / last` — the gate's own fraction ALONG THE LEG — and on the destination's half after it (EXECUTED `cure/D-PR-r3-probe/arith.out` (4): the leg-fraction test and the hop-local test agree at every tick of a four-node leg). `cut01` is 0.5 (DRAFT) until the digest carries a gate fraction — the digest's gates today are `{ between, cellA, cellB, cost }`, no fraction (a map-side slot the fold's INBOX records; cells are optional placement data, never asserted). A sea hop (`classifyLegModes(digest, path)[i] === 'sea'`) has no half: water carries no watch (PR15).

`roundOf(worldState, snapshot, settlementId, tick) → [{ half: { road: [a, b], holder: seat }, priority, cadence }]` — the town's ROAD HALVES: its own half of `road{seat, n}` for every digest neighbour `n` whose territory meets the seat's; aspatially the relationship-graph edges at the seat (one half each). A round never leaves the town's land, so a round never crosses a border (only a pursuit or an escort beyond the gate does — §10.1(c)). ORDERED by a fixed-order priority score (DRAFT weights, UNMEASURED): THE ARMY'S ROAD while the town's army is on campaign — the halves of the deployment's path out of the seat through the town's own land, its supply road, first of all (§3.2) · believed danger on the half (the town's belief of dens, menaces, bands and hostile units on it — §5.1) · its trade value (the caravan traffic the road carries: `commodityFlow`'s channel strength across it, the entrepôt toll it pays) · recent incidents (the town's in-horizon `patrol_report` receipts and banditry losses on it — and, where HB is lit (`habitConditioningEnabled`), weighted by the town's LEARNED HABIT of riding that stretch: HB's ONE curve on the settlement actor, credited when a report finds a `clash`, a `catch` or a `threat_seen` there (`habit/habitCurve.js :: applyCredit`, "`const step = learnRateFor(lawWord) * severityWeightOf(outcomeRung);`"), read through `habitFactor` ("An ABSENT stock returns EXACTLY the number one") as ONE bounded multiplier on the incident term, under HB's LANDED circumstance class `route_menaced` with the stretch key as its action token — recorded by ONE fold §3.1 HB INBOX row (the stretch-keyed action token and the report credit that feeds it), the term DARK ("the in-horizon count alone") until HB carries it (HB's row for `route_menaced` defines it by `embattlementLevel` and carries neither PR's credit producer nor a stretch token) — no thirteenth class (`habitVocabulary.js :: HABIT_CLASS_CEILING = 12`: "A thirteenth class is an owner gate, not an edit"), a town's single-digit halves inside `habitCurve.js :: HABIT_TUNING.HABIT_ROWS_PER_ACTOR_CAP` ("`HABIT_ROWS_PER_ACTOR_CAP: 24,`"); PLAUSIBLE until HB-0's car-0 admits a stretch-keyed token, and dark the incident term is the in-horizon count alone) ("a town raided from the hills learns to ride the hill road" — HB's one law, no second learner) · the WAYS the town KNOWS — ONE read, called by name: THE BODIES' `knownTrailsOf(worldState, snapshot, seat, tick)` (BD §2.3: "the town's STORED learned set above ∪ its OFFICE'S WAYS") — each known way's JUNCTION halves in the town's land, the road stretches `hiddenWayEdges(worldState, digest)` names as the way's `junctions` (BD §5.2: "a corridor beside `a|b` meets its own stretch, a bypass meets the stretches at its two ends, and once the map names a junction its key is `junction:<id>` beside `stretch:<a|b>` under the card's WHERE `road (a, b)`"), gain the KNOWN-WAY term — the choke point ("The BELIEVED junctions (the ways a town KNOWS, §2.3) are the patrols' choke points: PR's round plans by them"); a way the OFFICE knows (`how: 'by_office'` — "known AND watched by the office's trade") puts its junction halves FIRST, as a fort's attachment does; a way known only to a node (`upTo`) counts the junctions its known prefix reaches. The round READS NO `watched` mark: BD's stored `watched` is the FACT the round leaves — the stage marks a stored known way `watched` through BD's one writer `bodies/hiddenWays.js` the week its riders stand on one of the way's junction halves (that week's `lived` row) and BD's writer drops the mark after a season with none (BD §2.3) — so the mark records the watch and never feeds the priority that placed it (no loop) · a fort's attachment (ST §7.4's coverage row for PR — a held `fortification` at hand puts its halves FIRST on its holder's round; the riders who walk them are the holder's own, and the keep's GARRISON is the site's own complement whose road rows stand beside theirs, §10.5, OD-PR-41) · the DM's order (§11.1). The round's ORDER is derived each week; the week's resulting placement is written once (§2.7 `placement`). The brief's "beat" is the estate's NEWS BEAT (a bounded handful of beats; WC §7.C "The beat inventory"); the patrol's route is its ROUND, the state `on_round`.

**THE HIDDEN WAYS ON THE ONE LADDER — the words and the one read.** A hidden WAY is a corridor on master's route ladder at its lowest rung — `routeNetworkLedger.js :: ROUTE_GRADES` "`['highway', 'road', 'track', 'hidden']`"; in the map contract's words the rungs `wild · way · trail · track · road · highway`, the host's `hidden` the CLASS {`wild`, `way`} (`refs/MAP-REPLY-HIDDEN-WAYS-2026-10-18.md`) — read through ONE read, THE BODIES' `hiddenWayEdges(worldState, digest) → [{ trailKey, a, b, cover01, capacity, rung, weeksMult, junctions }]` — each a route-ledger edge in MODE `way` (`routeEdgeId(a, b, 'way')`, distinct from the road's `land` edge; `rung` its `wayRung` `wild · way · trail`, every rung ×2 and hidden) where the route lifecycle has lit it, else BD's candidate at the same price ("ONE read over ONE ladder … never a second network or price", BD §2.5), never a graph of PR's. `trail:<a|b>` is BD's corridor KEY ("a key, never a rung word", BD §2.3); PR's `trailKey`, `trailWatchOf` and "the trail arm" name that key and nothing else; the RUNG a way stands at is the route ledger's fact, never a PR word; DM-facing prose says "way" or "path" (the map's legend says the rung).

### 2.3 The cadence (per road half, per power) — the map's own four words, in the map's own meaning
`PATROL_CADENCES = weekly · every_four_weeks · every_thirteen_weeks · never` — exactly the host document's definition ("A *cadence* is how often a power's patrol passes a road stretch: weekly, every four weeks, every thirteen weeks, or never", V13 §3.9 (14)) and now its MEANING too — a PERIOD, never an average chance — so the two programs speak ONE vocabulary in ONE sense where the host's facts meet the map's lens in the window (§13). A half's cadence is DERIVED from RIDER-TIME — the `n` riders on the round this week (fielded minus those in detachments, §4.1) over the round's `H` halves in priority order — so that each rider stands on ONE place a week (PR1):
- `n = 0` — every half `never`.
- `n ≥ H` — every half `weekly`; the `n − H` spare riders double the round's first halves in order (two riders on one half read their summed band in `watch01`; overlap past that is the reserve).
- `n < H` — `r = min(n − 1, ⌈(H − n) / 3⌉)` riders ROVE and the other `s = n − r` STAND, so the round's FIRST half — the army's road on campaign, else the most dangerous or the richest (§2.2) — always has a standing rider: the first `s` halves are `weekly`, one standing rider each (a one-rider town walks its first half only); the remaining `H − s` halves are split, in priority order, into `r` consecutive blocks of near-equal size (earlier blocks the larger), one block per rover, and every roving half takes the rover period `p = 4` (`every_four_weeks`) when `H − s ≤ 4r`, else `p = 13` (`every_thirteen_weeks`), a block never holding more than `p` halves (any half beyond every block is `never`; with a town's single-digit halves, §0.4, a half is `never` only in a town with no rider on the round or one rider — EXECUTED `cure/D-PR-r3-probe/arith.out` (3): over `H` 1..9, `n = 1` leaves 36 of 45 halves `never`, `n ≥ 2` none). A rover walks ONE half of its block a week: its block's halves take SLOTS `0…k−1` ordered by `fnv1a32('patrol:slot:' + stretchKey + ':' + holder + ':' + power)` (codepoint tie-break), the rover's BASE is `fnv1a32('patrol:rover:' + power + ':' + blockIndex) mod p`, and in a week whose slot `(tick + base) mod p` holds no half (`k < p`) the rover is home (PR1's "home at rest"). DRAFT rule, UNMEASURED; PR-5 measures it on the soak corpus.
Presence in a given week (PR4): `onStretchThisWeek(half, power, tick)` = `true` for a `weekly` half; for a roving half `(tick + base) mod p === slot` (stored as `phase = (slot − base) mod p`, so presence is `tick mod p === phase`, §2.7); `false` for `never` — the week never enters a hash (`kernel/proseHash.js :: fnv1a32`, the zero-draw key hash the decision module `treatySuccessionDecision.js` already imports; `hashInt` is no landed helper). EXECUTED (`arith.out` (3), every `n` 0..12 × `H` 1..9 over 208 weeks): 0 weeks with more rider-places than riders, and every half present EXACTLY its word's period (`weekly` 208/208 · `every_four_weeks` 52/208 · `every_thirteen_weeks` 16/208) — the map's meaning of the words, kept.
THE CLOCK IS LEARNABLE, AND THAT IS ITS PRICE (L-BD 39 (b), the owner's "the patrol in their pursuit and evasion"). Presence is periodic and fixed by address (PR4; the host document's law "Patrols by address and week, never a roll"), so a watcher that has seen the riders on a half twice can name its period and phase: every court's `believedWatch` (§8.3) and a body's own habit record (THE BODIES, on HB's body grain) read that BELIEVED phase — "The Hollow Road gang has learned Kell's riders come on the fourth week" (scrutiny) — and a body plans its raid between the visits. PR adds NO seeded variation to the round (a roll would break PR4 and the host's law): the round's VARIATION is the world's own — a change of the count re-allocates at once, a believed threat or a hunt pulls riders off, the dwell re-buckets a reordered round, a lent rider or the crown's riders change a half's watch, and the DM's `PATROL_ORDER` breaks a rut (§11.1); a habitual round is caught out, which is the habit law's price ("being known is the price", HB).
THE DWELL damps re-bucketing at a CONSTANT count: when the round's priority reorders but `n` is unchanged, the newly derived allocation is held as a CANDIDATE (each changed half's `pendingCadence` and `pendingSince` in the placement digest, §2.7) and the allocation switches WHOLE — every changed half in the same week — once the candidate has held `CADENCE_DWELL` weeks unchanged (DRAFT 4, UNMEASURED), so a half-switched allocation can never put one rider in two places; a FIRST-RESPONSE change (war at home, a believed threat on a half, the army's road, the DM's `first`) or a CHANGE OF THE COUNT (up by first response; down by a levy, a loss, the pay or the will) re-allocates AT ONCE (the candidate is cleared), so the dwell never slows a war and no cadence word ever promises a rider the town does not field. The dwell keeps quiet years quiet and never slows a war. The host ASKS the map to draw its PRESENCE as sent rather than recompute it from the cadence word (the map's own phase is keyed from the road segment's lineage, so two clocks would part); the map program decides (§13). The brief's count band `none · thin · fair · strong` is not minted — `thin` and `strong` are MN's might words on the same cards, `thin` is also `RESERVE_BANDS`' and `fair` PS's tolerance band; the count is an integer (never stored) and a stretch's coverage speaks the map's cadence words.

### 2.4 The states
| State | What the patrol is doing this week | Covers its stretch? |
|---|---|---|
| `on_round` | walking the road half its round's clock placed it on (the placement, PR4) | yes |
| `converging` | riding toward a believed threat or a muster point (a move of more than one hop) | no |
| `engaged` | in a detachment at the threat: clearing, screening, pursuing | the stretch it stands on |
| `escorting` | riding beside a caravan, a column under PS's watch, or an envoy of its town, through its own land | the stretches it rides |
| `looking` | checking out a stressor or an authored event (§9.2), one week | the place looked at only |
| `resting` | at home after a detachment's errand ran past the rest bound (§7.3) | no |

"Dispersed" is the EVENT that returns a detachment's riders to `on_round` (a receipt), never a state. The brief's six states had `dispersed` as a state; it is an event (and `dispersed` is a landed lifecycle stamp, `settlementLifecycleFirstClass.js`); `looking` answers the owner's fourth word.

### 2.5 The detachment
When several riders act together (§5.2) they form ONE detachment: `{ detachmentId, settlementId, members: [ordinal…], allies?: [{ settlementId, members }], band, aim, place, sinceTick, causedBy, pursuedRef?, leaderRef? }` — `aim ∈ DETACHMENT_AIMS = clear · screen · pursue · escort · look` (`look` is a detachment of one). Its band is the members' summed latent strength, banded on the same five words. An allied town's riders — or a fellow member's under the banner (§10.3) — who JOIN (§10.2) keep their own command inside it (two commands on one stretch, WC's mirror-the-leg idiom) and are credited separately. `pursue` serves TWO quarries, never more: PT's road WATCH (a `party_ambush`, PR19 — its target is the card's, never stored here) and a BODY (THE HUNT, §5.5) — whose ref is `pursuedRef: { kind: 'body', id }` (the sixth actor kind). `leaderRef: { kind: 'person', id: <wnpcId> }` names a HUNT'S LEADER where a mission is taken (L-BD 40, §5.5) — a REFERENCE only: PR never moves a person's record, and no verdict on the detachment ever touches the leader (no named fate). It is the leader THE MISSION'S CARD names (BD's `hunt_taken`, §5.5) — the hunters' leader, never a BODY's: a body's leader has ONE record, CH's `paragon.leads`, which BD reads (`leaderOf`) and nobody copies. Both fields are drop-when-absent additions declared under OD-PR-45 (the owner's shape, visible). Tokens: `pursuedRef` and `leaderRef` FREE in `src` and `tests` (`cure/D-PR-r4-probe/tokens-r4.out`; `leaderRef` has one prose hit in a ledger doc, another surface); `quarry` (34 src files, 8 quoted literals — the stone quarry) and `leader` (42 src files) AVOIDED as bare names.

### 2.6 The derived reads (signatures; all pure; all in leaves under `src/domain/patrols/`)
- `patrolEnvelopeOf(worldState, snapshot, settlementId) → { ceiling, pool, pay, will, max, fielded, band, reasons[] }` (§3).
- `patrolsOf(…)`, `roundOf(…)` (above); `onStretchThisWeek(half, power, tick)` (PR4, the periodic clock over the rider-time allocation, §2.3); `patrolPlacementOf(worldState, settlementId, tick)` — the week's ONE placement every consumer reads (the digest row whose `forTick` is `tick`, §2.7); no consumer derives a placement of its own.
- `coverageOf(worldState, snapshot, settlementId, tick) → coverage01` — THE SLOW SIGNAL: the mean of the town's last four weekly coverages, read from the placement digest's ring (each week's share of its round walked, weighted by priority, × quality — band, pay, fatigue) (§6.1, §6.2).
- `watchOn(worldState, snapshot, roadKey, tick) → watch01` — THE DANGER READ the danger leans call: the MAX over every power whose riders stand on either half of the road this week (from the placement) of `quality(band, pay)` — one coverage per road, overlap is waste (item 12(f)) — 0 when none.
- `watchOf(worldState, snapshot, half, tick, power) → watch01` — THE PER-POWER READ, keyed by the HALF (`{ road: stretchKey, holder }`): that power's riders' quality on that half this week, 0 when its riders are not on it. The encounter runs once per power present on the traveller's half (§8.1); the smuggling lean reads the gate town's riders on its own half (§6.4); the witness reads the holder's on the holder's half (§6.5).
- `holderEyesOf(worldState, snapshot, half, tick) → watch01` — THE HOLDER'S EYES on its own half: the MAX of `watchOf(…, half, tick, half.holder)` (its own riders) and `watchOf(…, half, tick, p)` for every power `p` whose NET row stands on that half this week (§2.7: "the net's rows on a fellow member's half are this power's half rows keyed by that holder" — placed only where the holder answers `right`, §10.3 (d)); 0 when neither. ONE read for the two consumers PR10 names — the witness (§6.5) and the trail arm (`trailWatchOf`, below) — and NEVER the gate's catch (the smuggling lean reads the holder's own riders only: J385, §6.4) nor the encounter (which runs once per power, §8.1).
- `patrolEncounter({ worldState, snapshot, half, traveller, tick, power }) → { met, noticed, by, reasons[] }` — one call per power on the half (§8.1).
- `witnessedCrossing(worldState, holderId, gateKey, unit, tick) → boolean` (PR10; PS8's read; `gateKey = road{prev, B}`, §2.2).
- `believedWatch(worldState, snapshot, observerId, roadKey, tick) → { watch01, known }` — what a court BELIEVES the watch on a road to be, from NAMED producers (§8.3): its own riders' `riders_seen` reports at a shared gate, its allies' exchanged reports, its spies' product, rumor of a clash or a catch on the road (the travellers' return receipts are no producer until a mover's owner carries them, §8.3); with nothing heard it returns the TIER-REFERENCE PRIOR of the road's holder (the coverage a funded town of that tier fields at peace) with `known: false`, and the card says "unknown" — the ONE planning read. Its return is `{ watch01, known, phase? }` — `phase` the BELIEVED period and slot where two or more sightings on the half fix them (§2.3's learnable clock; L-BD 39); a TRAIL key `trail:<a|b>` is read too: `watch01` is the believed watch of the way's junction halves (the stretches `hiddenWayEdges` names as its `junctions`, §2.2) where the observer believes the holder KNOWS the way (it has heard of a catch on it, or of a warden holding the holder's public-security office — §5.5), else 0 with `known: false` — never the holder's true knowledge; and for a BODY observer the read is BANNER-BLIND (the MAX over every power it has heard of on the road, whoever's riders they are — "A BODY sees one banner").
- **A RIDING GANG READS ONE BAND WIDER.** THE MOVEMENT GRAMMAR's mount (MG §8.5: a BODY's raid that takes mounts makes the gang RIDE — "faster on road grades …; wider believed reach — PR's `believedWatch` reads a riding gang's reach one band wider") enters this read as ONE bounded step: where the subject on the road is a body carrying BD's `riding` status (`riding?: { sinceTick }` on the BODY record, OD-MG-7 the owner's, BD's field), its believed reach is read one band wider than its unmounted band — no new store, no new producer; DARK until MG's `ridingEnabled` and BD's field land; which argument of the read carries the widening is PR's to fix at that unit (PLAUSIBLE).
- `trailWatchOf(worldState, snapshot, power, trailKey, tick) → watch01` — THE ONE COVERAGE READ ON A HIDDEN WAY (BD §5.2): `known = knownTrailsOf(worldState, snapshot, power, tick).get(trailKey)` — THE BODIES' ONE known set, called by name ("PS's `hiddenWayFor`, PR's `trailWatchOf` and CH's routing all call it"); where `known` stands, the MAX over the way's JUNCTION halves in the power's land that its known prefix reaches (the `junctions` of `hiddenWayEdges(worldState, digest)`, §2.2; `known.upTo` bounds a partial knowledge) of `holderEyesOf(…, { road: junction, holder: power }, tick)`, else 0 — the power's EYES (its own riders, or a net rider by right, above) on a junction half this week, the week's ONE placement (R30) being the watch, so the stored `watched` mark is never re-read for coverage (it is the fact this placement writes, §2.2); ZERO on a way the power does not know (BD §5.2: "a patrol's coverage on an UNKNOWN way is ZERO; on a known WATCHED way it is the round's coverage at the junctions"); the office's ways count whole and watched by the office's trade (BD §2.3 `by_office`).
- `predictedPlaceOf(worldState, snapshot, settlementId, bodyId, tick) → { place, confidence, reasons } | null` — THE HUNT'S PREDICTION (§5.5), on BELIEF only: the town's ONE belief record of the body, BD's `believedBodyOf(worldState, townId, bodyId) → { known, mightBand?, baseId?, lastWhereId?, lastWhat?, lastTick?, leaderRef?, source }` (BD §2.4: "DERIVED ON READ from the town's rumor records carrying the body's id … and from the SIGHTINGS registered as rows of the same source" — a site's sighting, a patrol's report, "an INFORMER's word where the commons fear the body", a caught operative's confession), the ways the town knows (`knownTrailsOf`), and the body's habits (HB's body grain, else BD's `BODY_HABITS` as the kind's prior); `null` when the town has nothing to go on. One read for every mover that follows the hunt (the detachment; a paragon leader's own mover, CH).
- `bannerCoverOf(worldState, memberId, tick) → { weeks, seasonStartTick, swept }` — THE BANNER'S SEASON LEDGER read (§10.3 (f); ⭐ OD-PR-50: "The banner's record carries a per-member seasonal tally `weeksCovered` (reset at the season; a one-field addition, declared) that PL F.4/P49's protection rows read at the season's end — never the one-week `lived` rows"): the member's `weeksCovered` pair (§2.7) — the weeks of the CURRENT season (`seasonStartTick = tick − (tick mod INTERVAL_WEEKS.one_season)`) in which a rider of a power other than the member's own stood PLACED on at least one of its road halves — `weeks` 0 when the pair is absent or of another season; and `swept`, the weeks of the same season in which a rider of the CROWN'S OWN power (the member's polity root, never a fellow member's) stood placed on one of the member's halves FOR A NEED THE MEMBER'S OWN RIDERS COULD HAVE MET — the member's own riders home that week (no summons answered with riders), its envelope with riders to spare (`patrolEnvelopeOf(…).fielded < max`: the need was within its reach), the root at no war that week (a war road is the crown's own need), and the member not having asked (`patrol_joins`, §10.1 (d)) — 0 likewise; PL's protection rows read `weeks`, its precedence friction reads `swept` (PL §4.7 F.4, F.6 (b) (i)) (vetoable: "a need the member's own riders could have met" is READ as the four derived facts above, so no need tag is stored) (EXECUTED `cure/D-PR-r5-probe/season-r5.out` (a): a net that rode weeks 1–10 and slipped reads 10 weeks and `protection_kept` at the season's end; a span read from the one-week `lived` rows wrote `protection_failed`). The LOYALTY STACK is PL's BANNER STACK (PL §2: `polityOf(…, homeId).path` with `crownReachAt` at each rung, CUT at the first `swornLinkSuspended` link — "NO NEW READ"), and whether a crossing is BY RIGHT is PS's `passageStandingOf(…).banner` (`right · tolerance`; PS §5.5) — PR keeps no read of either.
- `patrolDrawOf(worldState, snapshot, settlementId, keptRiders) → points` and `patrolDrawnCap(worldState, snapshot, settlementId, cap) → { cap, drawShare01, kept, points }` — read ONCE per opener pass, right after `fromCap`, by the opener's feasibility gates, its `CONQUEST_MARGIN` prefilter and the seed (PR3; §6.6); `points` is the strength ACTUALLY drawn, `min(patrolDrawOf(…), cap.offensive)`, and `cap` is `fromCap` itself (the same object, byte-identical) when nothing is drawn — nothing kept, or nothing to draw from — so `drawShare01` is 1 there by construction and is taken only over a positive offensive, with its 0/0 guard reading 0 (§6.6).

### 2.7 The persisted shape — ONE ledger, four keyed maps (`spatialLedgers.patrols`, drop-when-empty)
The ledger nests under `spatialLedgers` (the FP-R namespace: "ZERO eager first-paint bytes", the `armyTransit` precedent) and is ABSENT whenever it would be empty.

| Map | Key | Row (closed fields) | Bound |
|---|---|---|---|
| `detachments` | `detachmentId = fnv([settlementId, aim, placeKey, sinceTick])` | §2.5's fields + `weeksOut` (the optional `pursuedRef` — a body — and `leaderRef` — a person — included) | at most `count` per town (a rider is in one detachment); dropped at dispersal (the receipt keeps the fact) — a hunt also at its cold trail (§5.5) |
| `losses` | `settlementId` | rows `{ tick, band, causedBy }`, newest first — each a `cut_down` verdict or default, the only way riders are lost (an absorption into the levy writes nothing) | ≤ 4 rows per town; a row stops counting at its RECOVERY (DRAFT one season, `INTERVAL_WEEKS.one_season`) and is dropped at write past two seasons (requirement 23's horizon) |
| `orders` | `settlementId` | `{ place?, priority? ('first'·'avoid'·'clear'), wages? (FUNDING_BANDS member), setTick, outcomeRef }` — the DM's overlay (§11.1) | one per town; cleared by the DM (`priority: 'none'`, `wages: 'derived'`) |
| `placement` | `settlementId` (the riders' power) | `{ next: { forTick, halves: { [stretchKey]: { [holder]: { cadence, since, phase?, on, pendingCadence?, pendingSince? } } } }, lived: { forTick, on: [{ road, holder }…] }, coverage: [c₋₃, c₋₂, c₋₁, c₀], forkSince?, weeksCovered? }` — `weeksCovered` THE BANNER'S SEASON LEDGER for this town AS A MEMBER: the pair `{ seasonStartTick, weeks, swept? }`, `weeks` the count of weeks of the season beginning `seasonStartTick` (`tick − (tick mod INTERVAL_WEEKS.one_season)`, PL's season boundary) in which a rider of ANY OTHER power stood placed on at least one of this town's road halves — counted by the stage at step 5 from every power's `lived` row of the week just lived (an entry `{ road, holder }` whose holder is this town on another power's row: a round never leaves its own land, §2.2, so every such entry is THE NET's), ONE count per week however many riders; `swept` (absent when 0) counted at the same step by the four facts of `bannerCoverOf` (§2.6: the root's own rider, the member's riders home with riders to spare, the root at no war, no ask — every one a derived read of that tick) — one sub-field in the same declared pair (⭐ OD-PR-50); the first counted week of a season REPLACES the pair (never summed across seasons); read only through `bannerCoverOf` (§2.6) by PL's protection and friction rows; ⭐ OD-PR-50, the owner's shape, declared; `forkSince` the first tick of the CURRENT unbroken run of weeks in which the town was at peace with nothing believed near and its peace-posture fork read `peaceShare > PEACE_SHARE` (§3.2), the one carrier of §7.5's "held one season" (written at the run's first week, kept while it holds, deleted the week it breaks) (OD-PR-44: declared on the placement row with its lifecycle — the owner's "held for a season" intent kept, a one-field addition to `spatialLedgers.patrols` declared as the owner's shape); the net's rows on a fellow member's half (§10.3 (d)) are this power's half rows keyed by that holder, `weekly` while their need stands — THE WEEK'S ONE PLACEMENT: the stage at tick t writes `next` for t+1 (each half's cadence word, the tick it took that word, a roving half's `phase` — the residue `tick mod p` it is walked in, stored so the held allocation stays exact through the dwell — whether the riders stand on it that week, and the dwell's CANDIDATE word with the tick it was first derived; the half is keyed by the stretch and its holder, the crown's riders on a member's half included) and moves the old `next` to `lived`; every consumer in tick t+1 reads the row whose `forTick` is its tick; the window after the advance reads `lived` (the week just lived — never a week ahead: "a marker is a presence, never an intention"); `coverage` is the four-week ring, one number a week | one row per town fielding riders — or carrying a season tally (a member whose own riders went to the levy keeps the row for its `weeksCovered` alone, `next`/`lived`/`coverage` then absent); a town fielding none and carrying no tally has no row (drop-when-empty); the ring holds four numbers, never more; the tally one pair, one season; machinery — never shown ahead of its week |

**Persisted shapes: ONE new (four maps of one ledger)** — with THREE drop-when-absent fields (`forkSince` on `placement`; `pursuedRef` and `leaderRef` on `detachments`), each DECLARED AS THE OWNER'S SHAPE and visible for his veto (OD-PR-44 · OD-PR-45; their lifecycles §16.1), and a fourth (`weeksCovered` on `placement`, ⭐ OD-PR-50, the owner's shape, declared; its lifecycle §16.1). **Rows added to landed shapes (no new shape):** ONE conditional stamp on the landed deployment record — `patrolDraw: { riders, points }`, written by ONE conditional line in the opener beside the seed WHENEVER THE COURT KEEPS RIDERS (`if (drawn.kept > 0) seededRecord.patrolDraw = { riders: drawn.kept, points: drawn.points };` — the landed idiom of the line after it, "`if (coalitionDecision) seededRecord.joinLedger = [coalitionDecision.anchor];`"; `seedDeploymentState`, `warArmyRecord.js` 72 eff, is not edited) (the W-C1 receipts' "stamp ONLY when non-trivial" idiom — kept riders ARE the non-trivial fact; absent ⇒ the record is byte-identical, as it is dark and for a court that keeps none), read by the stage to bound the riders fielded while that army is out (§3.2) and by the siege's Why line, validated fail-closed by its own block in the deployment normalizer (§16.1); the `patrol_report` feed entries with their typed content (`{ kind: 'patrol_report', finding, roadKey? (a stretch key), power?, band?, subjectRef? }`, `finding ∈ threat_seen · crossing_seen · riders_seen · found_nothing · catch · clash`; the rumor ledgers' own lifecycle); PS's crossing rows written with `witnessed` from PR's read (PS's shape); ONE `MOVEMENT_SITES` row (a test manifest); the registry rows of §11.2 (SP-9's ledger); the realm-manifest row of `PATROL_ORDER`.
