A catch on a trail teaches the town the trail (`by_catch`, BD's writer) | PR (the card) · BD (the body) |

**Nuances kept as rows (item 15(d)):** a spy from a home with a strong underworld passes a routine stop more often (ES's `covertCompetence01`, stance `stranger`); the same spy at war, on a watched road, under a tower, does not (× 1.3 × 1.3, cover only its competence); a grouped detachment notices more (its band); a patrol away notices nothing.

### 8.3 PLANNING IS BELIEF — ONE believed-watch read for every planner
Before any journey the traveller's OWN court plans on what it BELIEVES about the roads ahead: `believedWatch(worldState, snapshot, observerId, roadKey, tick)` — never the foreign town's truth. ITS PRODUCERS, named: (1) its OWN riders at a shared gate see the neighbour's riders on the neighbour's half — a `patrol_report` with `finding: 'riders_seen'` (§9.3; PR's own producer); (2) its ALLIES' riders' reports, exchanged at the gate (§10.1(b)); (3) its TRAVELLERS' RETURN — NOT A PRODUCER until a volume carries it (no mover's owner — the errand spine's envoy picture, a caravan's factor — carries the row in its own text, and no fold row records it; it returns by a declared row in that owner's volume, never by PR's text alone); (4) ES's PRODUCT — a spy at a foreign town reports the watch on its roads (ES's product row, ES's commit); (5) RUMOR of a clash or a catch on the road (a travelling `patrol_report`). Each belief ages toward the prior over the rumor's own TTL; with nothing heard the read returns the TIER-REFERENCE PRIOR of the road's holder with `known: false`, and the card says "unknown". ONE read, consumed in each planner's commit (a registration, never a second planning law): ES's mission planning (the dispatch refusal already reads BELIEVED danger, ES HZ9), the errand spine's route, CH's leg choice, EC-18's open road, WY's caravans (timing a departure, §6.3), THE PASSAGE's planner (the junction stretch of a trail the mover knows — a registration in PS-4's commit, the PS→PR route) and THE BODIES' route choice (BD12: "through THE PATROLS' ONE believed-watch read") — for which the read takes a TRAIL key too (the believed watch of the trail's junction where the body believes the holder knows the trail — a catch it heard of there, a warden it has heard holds the holder's office — else 0), returns the BELIEVED PHASE of a periodic half (`phase`, §2.3's learnable clock — "ridden every four weeks → raid in between", L-BD 39), and is BANNER-BLIND: a body composes every power's riders it has heard of on the road, whoever's they are (THE BANNER, §10.3 (f): a body sees one banner — deterrence by belief, never by ownership). PT does NOT consume it — the DM sees all, and PT's reachability card is the DM's read, not a court's belief (PT's own patrols line). What a planner does with a believed-watched road: AVOID it (the route re-scored with the believed watch as a cost beside danger — a `scoreRoute`-shaped term in the planner's own leaf, DRAFT weight), go ROUND it (by a hidden way — master's hidden rung, read through THE BODIES' `hiddenWayEdges`, open only to the movers master's franchise admits — `HIDDEN_PATH_KINDS`' covert envoy, smuggler and wanderer, and by declared changes a body and, in war, a small unit (BD §5.2) — at the landed hidden price ×2 (a beast ×1), size-capped at `modest`, met only at a junction of a way the holder knows; never a caravan, which takes a longer road at its weeks), or RISK it in haste (urgency, receipted — "the envoy would not wait"). Wrong belief sends an envoy into a patrol its court never knew of; a deceiver can make a road LOOK watched or empty (IN's plant road). The card says which in words ("Kell's envoy took the hill road; Kell did not know Ashford's riders were on it").

**The road catch's vocabulary** is ES's landed `caught · uncaught` — the `spy_at_target` kind with WHERE `road` (ruled, OD-PR-15); the fold brackets ES §3.3's J-ES-1 for the owner's fifth word.

## §9 FIRST RESPONDERS AND FIRST TO LOOK (the owner's third and fourth words; items 13, 14)

### 9.1 When war breaks out (item 13)
A war "breaks out" for a town the week any of these is first true: a live war front naming it (a declaration), an enemy army witnessed in its land (§6.5; PS's crossing rows) or on a stretch of its round, PS's `armed_incursion` opened in its land, a menace believed approaching (MN's court picture, §5.1).
- **(a) NOTICE.** The riders' `patrol_report` is the town's FIRST BELIEF of the war's shape — believed size (the army's band as witnessed), direction, the gate crossed — a hop earlier than the war's own front reads; it is the war's FIRST-VISIBLE sign in that town (requirement 16), and it lights PS's ONE fourth readiness signal `armsNear` on the town a week early (`martialReadiness.js :: threatEnvironment01`; PR is that signal's second producer, never a fifth signal — composed with THE PASSAGE's producer by PS §7.4's ONE composer `armsNearOf`: MAX, `THREAT_ARMS` added ONCE, PS §7.4 the one writer's home; PR registers its report row and writes nothing of the signal itself; the pin: exactly 0 when no passage crossing is live AND no report stands); the readiness term is `armsNear`, never the brief's "`raidHot`" (`raidHot` is built from war-type STRESSORS' affected towns; a believed approaching host is PS's `armsNear` in kind).
- **(b) SCREEN AND DELAY — an army marching on the riders' OWN town, or crossing its land toward another.** A detachment with aim `screen` meets the van on a half of the round. The van does NOT stand for the screen's hearing (unlike PS's border hearing, the riders are not the holder's gate answering a request): the card is heard in its week, and its VERDICT applies the screen's ONE week once — on `screened` or `cut_down` the screen leaf, at the head of the next transit pass and before the arrival check (its seal read first), asks THE ONE HOLD HELPER `holdBeforeNode` for its FREEZE mode — the unit held EXACTLY where the prior tick left it, one week, no refund (PS §4.1 owns the helper, its two modes and their arithmetic; PR keeps none of its own) — never the border clamp, which serves only a node the unit passed this tick (a screen's contact is mid-hop, where the clamp would move the van FORWARD); on `withdrew` nothing is held. At a ONE-WEEK advance the card is docketed and the hold lands at the next advance; at a MONTH, SEASON or YEAR advance the default resolves in its own tick and holds the same one week — never two at either length. Where THE PASSAGE is lit, the week counts INTO the march's ONE cumulative hold cap (`PASSAGE_HOLD_CAP_WEEKS = SIEGE_MAX_AGE − MAX_MARCH_WEEKS − 1`, DERIVED; PS9's Bound 2 — every hold of one march, passage, fort or screen, shares it); where THE PASSAGE is dark no `marches` row exists and the helper bounds the screen's hold by Bound 1 alone, the termination law (PS9's Bound 2 binds ONLY where THE PASSAGE is lit) — so the termination bound holds either way; the unit's KEY is its deployment id, stable across the held week (never `departTick`), so PS's crossing rows and the screen's `causedBy` keep one key. The screen's CARD is PR's wherever it is fought — on the approach to the riders' own town, or on its land as an enemy crosses toward a third town while PS's `armed_incursion` hears the holder's answer and keeps the home-defence battle (PR hosts the SCREEN card, PS1; PS keeps the home-defence battle — OD-PR-19). **Host:** PS's THE TRANSIT LEAF STAGE (`transitLeafStage.js :: TRANSIT_LEAVES`, a closed registration list; PS §4.1) gains `patrolScreenLeaf` in THE ONE ORDER OF REGISTRANTS, written once in PS §4.1 and cited here, never restated with arithmetic: PS's border leaf → THE SITES' approach-fort leaf → THE PATROLS' screen leaf → WC Arc A's `armiesMeetLeaf` (one hold per record per tick, the first to hold wins) — it inherits PS's car-0 and its STOP (S-a): if the transit kernel cannot be relieved, NO screen exists (the notice still does). The contest is `patrol_engages` (§11.2) — `screened` (held them a week), `cut_down` (held them a week and died doing it), `withdrew` (rode home with the word; no delay). A real army brushes a patrol aside: the odds of `cut_down` rise with the army's band over the detachment's (DRAFT, one keyed draw); a held `fortification` or `crossing` site on the stretch (ST) shifts the default toward `screened` (an anchor to hold). The levy's week is the town's to use (ST item 17: the ready keep is the first line of defence; the riders buy the week on the roads beside it, never as its garrison; WC-5's call-ins go out). Why the screen is PR's own hearing (§0.5 R-11): PS1 makes the DESTINATION's land never a passage, so an army marching on its target meets no PS card in the target's land; an enemy crossing a THIRD town's land is PS's `armed_incursion`, and the riders' screen of it is PR's own card beside PS's.
- **THE SCREEN IS A UNIT-SIGHTING PRODUCER TOO.** The van a `screen` detachment meets is SEEN — ONE `unit` sighting with its `heading?` through MN's `recordSighting`, exactly as §9.2 (b) states for the look (PR's look and screen are among the declared unit-sighting producers); the hold stays the army's alone — the screen decides on the ARMY record it knows and names no wing; THE MOVEMENT GRAMMAR's stepper carries the screen's FREEZE to that army's wings (MG §3.2 step (0) (b); PS, ST and PR carry no wing text).
- **(c) SHELTER AND WARN.** Riders TURN BACK caravans on the threatened stretches (the stretch's believed watch reads hostile for the town's own movers — §8.3's avoid), ESCORT refugee columns away from the front (§10.4; POP's columns), and WARN the villages (the report's news line; the flight read follows the danger ledger by construction once the enemy feeds it). PS's enemy-column presence feeder is WIDENED to the destination's own land (one feeder, PS's commit), so the target's danger — and the landed flight — rise as the host approaches; PR adds nothing (PLAUSIBLE until PS's text carries it).
- **(d) THE BORDER FORTS.** A held fort is already garrisoned — by the SITE's own complement, paid through its holder by the site's funding band, never by the holder's riders (ST10; OD-PR-41); in week one a fort on a stretch of the round anchors the screen (above). PR adds no garrison and draws none.
- **(e) THE ARMY'S ROAD, THE REAR, AND WHAT IS KEPT.** When the levy is up and the army marches, the court keeps its campaign count by default — never below its own peace count, one more wherever `max` allows (§3.2) — and those riders take the army's road out first (§3.2); the riders not kept fold into the field force, and the halves the kept riders cannot reach — the back roads, the rear — go unpatrolled: the slow half fades over four weeks, the counter term turns negative (§6.2), and brigandage rises in the rear and dens grow behind the lines (ST's dens and THE BODIES' gangs, by construction — BD §9.8 (a): "WARTIME IS THE GANGS' SEASON — the levy takes the riders (PR's fielded count falls, so security falls and habitat rises)") — except on the stretches a held, funded keep watches with its own garrison, whose rows stay (OD-PR-41). A chooser that KEEPS MORE riders keeps its rear safe and marches a smaller army — the draw taken at that deploy (`patrolDraw`, §6.6): ONE bounded chooser signal through THE CHOOSER'S SHARED-SIGNAL CAR — WAR CIRCULATION's owned `WC-car-0 THE CHOOSER'S SIGNAL CAR`, cut before EC-17 as the first claimant's prerequisite, PR a REGISTERED CLAIMANT — the claimant programs are SEVEN (fold §3.1.23 and OD-FC-59): EC-17 · MN-4/MN-5 · PL-3/PL-4 · HB-7 · INT · PR-1's `keepRiders` · PS-4's `passageChooserTermOf`; the joint budget measured once, at WC-car-0's unit (fold §3.3.16); PR adds one term row, `keepRiders`, read from the court's risk posture — `strategicPosture.js`, requirement 10 — and the believed rear danger), read when the army is raised and not again until it is raised anew.
- **(f) COUNTERFORCES.** A deceiver's false report mobilises for nothing (§7.4); standing readiness costs wages (§7.1); a cut-down patrol is a loss — the deed, the tale and a season without riders (§7.2; no grievance input of its own); a menace's approach is met by a report and a withdrawal unless the riders group to its band (§5.3).

### 9.2 Checking it out (item 14)
- **(a) TRIGGERS — closed lists.** An APPROPRIATE STRESSOR in the patrol's territory: `INVESTIGABLE_STRESSOR_TYPES = monster_raider_pressure · criminal_corridor · insurgency · slave_revolt · infiltration` — each a LANDED stressor type key (measured against `STRESSOR_COUNTERFORCES`' and `STRESSOR_SPAWN_GATES`' keys: `siege famine occupation political_fracture indebtedness betrayal infiltration disease_outbreak succession_void monster_raider_pressure insurgency religious_conversion_fracture slave_revolt rebellion coup_detat wartime mass_migration market_shock criminal_corridor magical_instability magic_deadzone`); never famine, plague, a market shock or the arcane crises as such. The brief's "the raider row, crime, unrest and riots, a monster, a disappearance" maps onto the landed keys so: raiders and monsters are ONE type (`monster_raider_pressure`), crime is `criminal_corridor`, armed unrest is `insurgency`/`slave_revolt`; riots are the commons' ladder (`commonsVoiceKernel.js` `RUNG_RIOT`) inside the walls — the town watch's business, not the riders'; a disappearance has no stressor type and is an AUTHORED event (below). An APPROPRIATE AUTHORED EVENT: an Edit Mode / event-composer event (`components/settlement/eventComposer/buildEvent.js`) whose type is on `INVESTIGABLE_EVENT_TYPES` (DRAFT: the composer's harm and disorder events — the institution events PT maps, a raid, a disappearance — named at car-0 from the composer's catalog) — investigable BY DEFAULT, the DM switches the look off per event (a boolean on the authored event's own payload, `noLook: true`, the edits-delta idiom; its lifecycle §16.1, ⭐ OD-PR-53); THE DOMAIN CARRIER of "an authored event in the territory this week", named at PR-2's car-0 as a measured seam: a DM event authored on a clock-bound member is QUEUED on `worldState.pendingEvents` and drained at the top of the campaign advance, BEFORE the pulse (`events/drainQueuedEvents.js :: drainQueuedEvents`: "They queue on the campaign (worldState.pendingEvents) and resolve together at the next world-pulse tick"), each drained entry landing on that member's `campaignState.eventLog` STAMPED WITH THE TICK ("worldState.tick, stamped on each drained entry", its JSDoc); the look reads THIS tick's drained entries of an investigable type without `noLook` — PLAUSIBLE until car-0 confirms the patrol stage's snapshot reaches the drained log (else the drain's `updates[*].authoredEvent` is threaded to the stage by the advance, one argument); a PARTY impact leaves traces a patrol may find (PT's deed at the place, §9.3's "the party's traces").
- **(b) THE LOOK.** ONE patrol leaves its round for ONE week (`looking`; the round's lowest-priority stretch loses its rider — the cost) and goes to the place (the light-unit physics; a look farther than one hop is a converging week first). It reads what is there BY THE WORLD'S OWN BELIEF RULES: a site's holder (ST; a site under the court veil — `keptSecret`, ST §2.4 — is found only with a keyed draw, `LOOK_FIND_SECRET` 0.3 — never `hidden`, ST's withdrawn word and THE BODIES' `hiddenWays` homonym; the name is free, `tokens-r4.out`), a menace's tracks (MN), a body (THE BODIES), the party's traces (PT's deed at the place in the last season, if its `proof` is `shown` or `spoken` — PT §11's visibility), or NOTHING. Seeded (stream `patrol:look:<settlementId>:<placeKey>:<tick>`). A FINDING IS A SIGHTING, written ONCE at the tick of seeing into the ONE belief of that holder's might — a row `{ id, mightBand, tick }` (the band seen then) INTO the looking court's own belief through MN's ONE door `menaceLedger.js :: recordSighting(worldState, { courtId, subject: { kind: 'menace' | 'body', id }, mightBand, tick, whereId, siteId })` — THE ONE SIGHTING STORE `spatialLedgers.sightings` keyed court × subject, MN's record, which MN's `believedMenaces` (a MENACE) and BD's `believedBodyOf` (a BODY) READ (PR's look and the at-hand witness write through it) — and a look at an ID-LESS AUTHORED GANG (no record and no belief owner before BD-0, ST §2.1) writes nothing (a sighting row `{ id, mightBand, tick }` is written INTO the sighting court's belief record, never onto ST's holder object; ST's writer's closed list holds no sighting input of THE PATROLS — ST: "a patrol's look writes into the holder's own belief record by its owner's door (MN's, THE BODIES'), never through ST's writer") (a patrol's look refreshes it — never a live truth read, never a second belief) — and a kept-secret den found by a look is the at-hand court's WITNESS; the veil itself is lifted only by ST's `made_known`, never by the riders; PR keeps no belief of its own.
- **THE LOOK IS A UNIT-SIGHTING PRODUCER** (one of the three DECLARED producers, with WC Arc A's contact and ES's product; MG §5.3–§5.4): a look that finds a MILITARY UNIT at the place (an army, a column, or a wing of THE MOVEMENT GRAMMAR) writes ONE sighting through MN's ONE door `recordSighting` with the subject kind `unit` (`subject: { kind: 'unit', id: <the unit key> }`) and the widened field `heading?` — the posture's `toward` while the unit FEINTS, else the next node of its true path (what the riders saw it march on) — the widening MN's row (⭐ OD-MG-6, THE OWNER'S persisted-shape row, VISIBLE); PR writes no belief of its own. Until this producer (or WC's, or ES's) lands, MG's feint, `believedHeadingOf` (bar the casus fallback) and `believedMotiveOf` are DARK, never invented.
- **(c) THE REPORT.** A `patrol_report` into the town's belief (§9.3); a CALL-BOARD entry where a need is found (CH §14's `callBoard` read gains the report as a source row in CH's commit — the den in the hills is a `wildland` call); and a ZERO-CLICK SUGGESTION to the DM ("Kell's riders report a den at the old fort — mark it?") riding ST's and PT's doors (batched, non-blocking suggestions) — never a decision made for the DM: the engine never places a site (ST3) and never writes a party deed.
- **(d) THE ACT, AT THEIR SIZE.** A small thing is handled on the spot (a band below the patrol's own band: §5.3's contest that week); a large thing is grouped against or withdrawn from with the report (§5.2).
- **(e) COUNTERFORCES.** A patrol away is a road unpatrolled (the round's cost); a planted false lead (IN) wastes it; a wrong look can be an ambush (a body stronger than the patrol at the place: `patrol_engages`, `cut_down` possible); a town with few riders investigates little and learns late.
- **(f) A QUARANTINE THE HOST SETS** (plague-R7): when PG's quarantine verb exists (the host's own verb with a stance input), the riders ENFORCE it — a stance `closed` for people at a gate makes the town's riders on that gate's stretch turn travellers back (§8.2's stop at the stance) — never stand in for it; until the verb lands, patrols hold no quarantine line (the host document: "a patrol is never drawn … holding a quarantine line").

### 9.3 The report and the deed
`patrol_report` is a RECEIPT kind (no hearing — it decides nothing between two parties) and a rumor FEED ENTRY: assembled in `migrationRumors.js :: rumorCarrierParams` beside the `migration_flight` lane (+≈6 lines through a leaf `patrols/patrolReports.js`; host 83), ingested by the ONE writer `advanceRumorLedgers` with `partyIds` = [the patrol's town] (its hop-0 witness — `RumorContent.partyIds` means the involved SETTLEMENTS) plus every ALLIED town whose riders walked the same stretch that week (§10.1(b)'s shared eyes — a hop sooner, no new carrier), magnitude by the threat's band (MN's might↔magnitude map), never the exact count. Its content is typed (§2.7): `finding ∈ threat_seen · crossing_seen · riders_seen · found_nothing · catch · clash` — `riders_seen` (a neighbour's riders seen on the neighbour's half at a shared gate) is the producer the planners' `believedWatch` reads (§8.3). Under infoMode `full` it travels and weathers as every telling does; under the news-only presets it is a news line; under the omniscient arm the town already knows (the report is the chronicle line only). **Deeds** (RN's door, never PR's writer): a catch of a named traveller, a cut-down patrol, a den cleared, a screen held — each a candidate `DEED_SOURCES` row PR names for RN's ENGINE-SOURCE slot — ONE ACT, ONE DEED, the hearing's owner minting: `patrol_clash` — an UNLED patrol's clash, DOER THE RIDERS' TOWN (`{ kind: 'settlement', id }`, a TALE: a court's deed moves no court's fame, for no court has fame — RN1); a patrol CUT DOWN by a NAMED body, DOER THE BODY with its leading paragon as `ledBy` (§7.2); a clash INSIDE A LED HUNT mints NONE — the mission's close is the hunt's one door (CH's `hunt_won`, doer the leader; the body's `escaped` where it fails — §5.5); `patrol_catch` — a catch of a named traveller, doer the riders' town, or the named paragon riding with them that week (§10.5; RN's row) — each minted by THE ONE PERSON STAGE from the verdict record (RN's rule); until RN opens the slot, the receipt is the record.

---

## §10 ALLIES, THE KINGDOM AND THE PASSAGE (the owner's second word; items 7, 8, 12)

### 10.1 Allied and kingdom patrols meeting (item 12)
- **(a) RECOGNITION IS BELIEF.** Two powers' riders on one stretch know each other as far as their towns' beliefs go: each reads `belief(own, other).allianceLabel` (truth when beliefs are dark). Allies and members of one polity (PL's walk, PL1) pass without contest — members while the rung between them stands; across a BROKEN rung (a feud, a break, a contest that suspends the link) recognition is lost and the pair stance decides (§10.3 (g)). A STALE belief — an alliance broken, a war begun this week and not yet heard — makes an incident between friends: one side believes `hostile`, the other `allied`, and the meeting is a `patrol_engages` card whose default is `withdrew` for the side that believes the other a friend (DRAFT; the stale side does not strike first unless martial). A provocateur (CH) stages one with a false report (§7.4).
- **(b) SHARED EYES.** Allied riders — and the riders of members under one unbroken banner — on a shared stretch EXCHANGE what they saw: each town becomes a hop-0 witness of the other's `patrol_report` from that stretch that week (§9.3) — a belief channel a hop sooner, never a new carrier; a dense allied net sees its whole land. Feuding members share no eyes (§10.3 (h)).
- **(c) JURISDICTION = PS's LADDER.** A patrol stepping onto a half BEYOND its own territory (past the digest gate — a round never does, §2.2; only a pursuit or an escort) is a military unit crossing and PS applies (`passageStandingOf`, PS4; PS's `PASSAGE_MOVER_KINDS` holds `patrol`, PS §2.1), with ONE lean: `passageSizeBand` of a lone patrol is `quiet` against almost any holder and PS's tolerance width for a `quiet` unit is spent at half a week per week (PS §4.4) — so an ally's patrol rides freely for weeks on end (a `fair` width lasts eight weeks for a `quiet` unit, a `wide` one a season; an ally's width RISES while it is itself at war). An ally in HOT PURSUIT (a detachment with aim `pursue` following a body across the border) is `welcomed` as urgency at an ally's gate (receipted); a stranger's patrol must ask like any unit (PS's `passage_at_the_border`), and a cool ally's uninvited patrol reads PS's own `tolerated` or `refused_with_warning`; an ENEMY's patrol (the rung `enemy`) is never `refused` — no request is possible — its placement on the far half opens PS's `armed_incursion` card, PS fights NO border battle for it (a patrol has no transit record), `barred` refuses the placement that week (PR re-places it), `unopposed` lets it ride under the standing card, and where the holder's riders stand on that half the meeting of riders with riders is PR's own `patrol_engages` (a believed `hostile`, (a), (f)) (PS §5.5). INSIDE A KINGDOM the line is THE BANNER's (§10.3 (d)): where the crown's own hold reads STRONG and the rung between the two members stands, a member's patrol rides a fellow member's roads BY RIGHT where PS's jurisdiction read answers `right` (`passageStandingOf(…).banner`: the crown's hold as the HOLDER believes it is `staunch` or `unbroken` — PS §5.5, OD-PS-45) — no tolerance spent; PS opens a `banner` permit and ONE card defaulting `granted` at the first placement; where it is WEAK, the fellow member's riders cross as an ally's on this paragraph's ladder, with the `quiet` unit's half-week spend; across a broken rung the believed label decides as for any unit. The charter grants tolls only (`toll_exemption`, PL §4.5), never leave to cross. (§0.5 R-9: members' riders never cross by a charter — a polity's charter grants a `toll_exemption` only (PL §4.5), and PS4 already makes members allies on the ladder; under a FRAYED hold members still ask, because a frayed member's believed label may not read ally.)
- **(d) GROUPING ACROSS TOWNS — `patrol_joins` (CLIMAX).** Against a believed threat bigger than either town's riders can reach alone, a town may ASK an ally whose land or round touches the threat's place to JOIN its detachment: a decided act of the ally's court, verdicts `rides · will_see · refused` — `rides` (its riders join under their own command, §2.5), `will_see` (it sends none this week but turns its own round toward the shared stretch — its coverage there rises; a lesser help, no detachment), `refused`. The default reads the LANDED RELATIONSHIP PAIR STATE between the ally and the asker in EVERY arm — label, trust and resentment (`relationshipState`, law E14; RN's refused pairs leave court↔court feeling to it) — `allied` with high trust rides, cool trust will see, resentment or a non-allied label refuses — and its own believed danger; RN's regard enters only through a PERSON decider (the ally's acting officer) if a later wave wants one. Credit: each town's riders are credited separately for a den cleared or a body driven off (WC-1's contribution idiom → regard through RN's door when lit; a court-to-court `contributionLedger` row when WC-1 lands); a refusal is remembered on the relationship record (the one relationship writer, a small resentment patch — DRAFT) — the writer named: ONE state-only relationship outcome `{ relationshipKey (the asking court ↔ the refusing one), relationshipPatch: { resentment: +PATROL_JOIN_REFUSED_RESENTMENT } }` (DRAFT, UNMEASURED, FP-46), its cause `patrol_join_refused` in the receipt, through `relationshipEvolution.js :: applyRelationshipPatch(worldState, outcome, now, edge)`, called DIRECTLY by the patrol stage on the worldState it returns (the stage returns no outcomes — the transit leaf's idiom, PS).
- **(e) THE HOME HAS FIRST CALL** on its own roads, and takes charge there (§10.3 (a)): the crown's riders and the net's lent riders fill only the halves a home leaves unwatched, never displacing its own (§10.3 (d)).
- **(f) FRICTION.** Coverage CAPS PER ROAD: `watchOn` is the MAX over the powers on a road, never the sum (overlap is waste) — the danger read; `watchOf` keeps each power's own for the encounter (§8.1). Members in a FEUD (PL §6) treat each other's riders as hostile on shared stretches: a meeting is `patrol_engages` (two settlements' units, requirement 24 in its plainest form), and a clash is the feud escalating — the liege's arbitration (`polity_arbitration`, PL §6) is the road out, never PR's. A den on a shared border is ONE contest (ST's rule): the ANCHOR court's detachment (the site's attachment) contests first; a second at-hand court's detachment that rides JOINS it as one force — the bands summed, the anchor named the clearer, the joiner receipted and credited (§10.1 (d)'s idiom) — and contests alone only where the anchor does not ride; never two contests.
- **(g) ESCORT HANDOVER AT THE GATE.** An escort (§10.4) rides to its town's border gate; the next territory's riders take the caravan if they stand on the gate's stretch that week and their town is not hostile to it, else it proceeds unescorted; the crown's riders escort end to end inside the kingdom.

### 10.2 Who asks whom
`patrol_joins` is asked only by a town whose own fielded riders cannot reach the threat's band (§5.2) — a hunt's included (§5.5) — only of allies or members, and at most one ask per threat per week (DRAFT; the next ask waits for the threat to grow) — so the docket carries a join only when one is needed. Under a strong banner a fellow member's riders who ride cross by right (§10.3 (d)); the JOIN itself stays that member's decision on this card — the banner spares the crossing, never the choice.

### 10.3 THE BANNER — every patrol in a kingdom (the owner's ~19:2x word, item 9 above: "every patrol in a kingdom is working under the highest overlord banner, and every subsequent level until it reaches them inteh vassal chain with a home local font thet is their home settlement"; PL §4.7 F.1–F.7; items 7, 12(e))

- **(a) ONE HOME, A STACK OF BANNERS (PL §4.7 F.1).** Every patrol has ONE home — the settlement that raises, pays and houses it: its envelope is the home's (§3), its round the home's (§2.2), and on the home's OWN halves the home has FIRST CALL (its riders are placed first; nobody's riders displace them, §10.1 (e)). Its LOYALTY STACK is PL's BANNER STACK — "NO NEW READ: a patrol's stack is `polityOf(worldState, snapshot, homeId).path` (home → … → paramount) read rung by rung with `crownReachAt` at each rung, and CUT at the first rung whose link `swornLinkSuspended` reads true" (PL §2, §4.7 F.1) — DERIVED at every read and NEVER STORED: when the chain changes (a fealty sworn, a break, a sale, a conquest, a contest) the banners change at the next read, and no patrol row holds a loyalty; PR mints no stack read of its own. A town in no polity has a stack of one, its own banner. On scrutiny a patrol's card shows its home and its stack as the chain ("Kell's riders — sworn to Tolle, under the crown of Merewick").
- **(b) THE CALL — NEVER SKIPPING A LORD'S ROW (PL §4.7 F.1, PL §4.1).** A lord's call on riders is PL's SUMMONS — the riders an input line on `call_in_answer` (OD-PL-50; (e) below: `answered` · `half_answered` keeps them on the member's roads · `refused`), a hearing with its fealty row. Under EVERY hold below `unbroken` the OVERLORD's call reaches a patrol rung by rung: the crown calls its direct vassals; each answers for its own riders and calls its own (PL §4.1: "the crown summons its direct vassals; each mid-lord answers for itself and summons its own"). Under `unbroken` — the crown's own read, `polityHoldOf(…, crownId, crownId)` (PL §4.1) — the crown MAY summon any member DIRECTLY: the riders' line goes to that member on that member's own `call_in_answer`, and EVERY LORD BETWEEN still has its ROW (PL §4.1 (ii), F.1: "every lord between still has its row — its credit when it lets the men go, the precedence friction when the crown rode past it"): where the lord itself answered the same call `answered` or `half_answered`, it LET its vassal's men go — the lender's yearly credit (PL F.6 (a): one `service_answered` row a year, episode `riders_lent:<rootId>:<yearStartTick>`, no patch); where the crown did not summon it in that call, or it answered `refused` or let the call lapse, the crown RODE PAST IT — the precedence friction, ONE `riders_quartered` row carrying ONE resentment patch (PL F.6 (b) (ii); §7.6), episode the call-in request's key; nothing is written for a lord between when the member refused. Every summons row sits on the answering member's OWN sworn edge, to its immediate liege, whoever called (PL §4.1 (i): "the vassal of my vassal is not my vassal"). PR mints none of these: they are PL's, read from the call's own answers.
- **(c) ONE SHARE LAW, AND A LEND THAT NEVER READS ZERO AT A STRONG HOLD (PL §4.7 F.2).** The liege's share of a member's RIDERS is the SAME share as its voice in every decision — PL's row (PL §4.7 F.2: "PL4's `POLITY_LIEGE_SHARE[band]` of the member's seat weight toward its IMMEDIATE liege, at every tier — never a second share for riders"; `marginal 0.10 · present 0.25 · strong 0.38 · dominant 0.45`), read at the FIRST rung of the member's BANNER STACK ((a); PL §2) — PR keeps no share of its own. Under the crown's own read `staunch` or `unbroken` ((d)) each member m LENDS up to `lend(m) = max(1, ⌊n(m) × s(m) × hold01⌋)` of its `n(m)` riders on the round — `s(m)` its band share toward its immediate liege, `hold01` the crown's own read (PL §3.1 (e)) — but only while it keeps at least THE HOME'S MINIMUM itself, `n(m) − lend(m) ≥ NET_HOME_MIN` (DRAFT 1, UNMEASURED — PR's constant beside the per-stretch cap: one rider always stays on the round's first half, §5.2's rule), else it lends none (a one-rider hamlet keeps its rider); a SUB-VASSAL lends through its LIEGE's line, up the chain rung by rung (F.1): its riders join the line its liege's banner carries, counted ONCE — never re-scaled at each rung and never computed at the crown's own share (PL F.2: "`crownReachAt`'s paramount reach is the crown's voice in a sub-vassal's DECISIONS, PL5, not a call on its men"). `lend(m)` is a CEILING: the net takes from m's round, in REVERSE priority, only the riders it PLACES on a need ((d)); the rest stay on the home's round. The crown's own riders join at `min(⌊n(root) × hold01⌋, n(root) − NET_HOME_MIN)` under `command: 'paramount'` ("the crown's riders" in prose, R-8), so a crown keeps first call on its own roads too (the home's minimum binds the root as it binds every member: at `unbroken`'s `hold01` 1.0 a crown would otherwise lend every rider it had — a vetoable refinement). The net is the Σ of the riders PLACED (each member's ≤ `lend(m)`) plus the root's, the per-stretch cap binding (PL F.2: "PL owns the share, the floor rule and the hold; PR the composition, the minimum and the cap"). EXECUTED `cure/D-PR-r5-probe/net-r5.out` (every tier × band × the hold's four ends, at the peace count and at `max`): no member with two or more riders lends 0 at a strong hold (0 rows), no lender keeps fewer than the minimum (0 rows), R39's Ashford (a town, three riders on the round, any band toward Kell) lends 1 at hold 0.60, ten town vassals at `strong` lend 10.
- **(d) THE LINE BLURS BY THE HOLD (PL §4.7 F.3).** Each decision of the banner has ONE owner and ONE read, never two reads of one fact. (1) WHETHER A CROSSING IS BY RIGHT is THE PASSAGE's jurisdiction read (for same-kingdom riders it reads the hold): `passageStandingOf(worldState, snapshot, holderId, riderHomeId, unit).banner` — `right` where the rider's home and the half's holder are members of ONE polity with no believed `hostile` between them and the crown's hold AS THE HOLDER BELIEVES IT (`polityHoldOf(…, rootId, holderId).band`) is `staunch` or `unbroken`, `tolerance` where it is `frayed` or `shaky`, nothing across a broken rung (PS §5.5, OD-PS-45 — PS3's law, "B answers on its own belief"; re-read at every placement); PR keeps NO read of its own for it. (2) WHETHER THE CROWN CALLS THE NET AT ALL — composes its riders and its members' lent riders across the kingdom — is the crown's decision on its OWN read (`polityHoldOf(…, rootId, rootId)` `staunch` or `unbroken`, PL §3.1 (e), the site that already decides the crown's riders); and HOW MANY of a member's riders it may lend is PL's one share times the crown's own `hold01` ((c)); the receiving member's right is its own belief (PL §3.1 (f), PS's read). Each decision reads its own decider's belief, PL §3.1's rule, and the right and the net never disagree: the net places no lent rider where the right is absent. Then:
  - **STRONG — THE KINGDOM IS ONE NET.** With the net called, a member's patrol rides a fellow member's roads BY RIGHT OF THE BANNER wherever that member answers `right` — a pursuit, an escort, a hunt, a join, a lent rider — with no tolerance spent: PS opens a `banner` permit and ONE `passage_at_the_border` card defaulting `granted` at the first placement on that member's land, no hold (PS's; the DM may still tilt or decide it, requirement 24). Coverage COMPOSES across members' borders on the shared stretches: after every home has placed its own round (§4.1 step 5), THE NET places the crown's riders on any member's halves and each member's lent riders (up to its `lend(m)`, (c)) on the fellow member's half of a road SHARED with its home's land — only on a half left unwatched this week (`never`), only where that half's holder answers `right` (a lent rider is never sent to spend an ally's width), and only where a NEED stands on it, in this order: (i) the member's OWN riders went to the levy (protection, (f)); (ii) a believed threat on it that the member's own riders cannot reach (§5.1–5.2); (iii) the crown's war roads and trade (the crown's own priority, §2.2's terms read for the root); (iv) in PEACE, only where the crown's own peace-posture fork keeps more riders out (`peaceShare(root) > PEACE_SHARE`, §3.2) — the crown's call on the net in peace, whose price is (h). The crown's OWN riders may still ride a half whose holder answers `tolerance` — as an ally's patrol on its width (the weak arm below, from that holder's belief). The net's rows are the lending power's half rows keyed by that holder, `weekly` while their need stands and dropped when it ends (a first-response change, §2.3); the per-stretch cap stops stacking (`watchOn` is the MAX, §10.1 (f)); the net never displaces a home's own rider. A net rider on a member's half WITNESSES for it (PR10) and counts on a known trail's junction (§2.6 `trailWatchOf`); the GATE's catch stays the holder's own riders' (J385, §6.4).
  - **WEAK — EACH LORD HIS OWN ROADS** (the crown's own read `shaky`/`frayed`). The crown composes NO net and quarters no riders: "its own riders keep to the root's land" (PL §4.7 F.3, §15.12); members' rounds stay their own; and any rider that does cross a member's border — a pursuit, an escort, a hunt, the crown's own included — crosses on PS's ladder as an ALLY's patrol (members stand as allies, PS4) with PR's ally size tolerance (a `quiet` unit spending at half a week per week, §10.1 (c)). The same holds at a single border whose holder believes the hold weak under a strong crown (PS's `tolerance`): no lent rider is placed there. `frayed` no longer means "members ignore the crown's riders": it is this arm.
  - **BROKEN** — recognition lost; see (g).
- **(e) THE CROWN'S CALL ON THE RIDERS AT WAR — PL's summons; no kind of PR's** (the call rung by rung, (b)). At war, the crown's call on a member's fighting men is PL-5's SUMMONS, answered on WC's `call_in_answer` (`answered · half_answered · refused · lapsed`; PL §4.1), and a member's riders are inside that levy (PR3) — so the riders ride the SAME card as ONE input line: `answered` — the member's riders go with its levy (those the crown's round can use ride the war's roads under `command: 'paramount'` for the war's season, credited like WC-1's contribution); `half_answered` — the member sends its levy and KEEPS its riders on its own roads (PL §4: "Half service — WC's `half_answered`; a `service_answered` row with the fraction"); `refused` — neither (PL prices it `service_refused` through its own writer, as for any refusal); `lapsed` — undecided. PR mints NO summons kind, no second vocabulary and no fealty row of its own, and PR itself requests no fealty row; the `patrol:` prefix PL registers in `FEALTY_EPISODE_PREFIXES` (PL's rule: riders and a retained company are units; harbouring them is priced) is PS's request, not PR's — a patrol as a passage MOVER (`unitKey = 'patrol:' + detachmentId`, PS §2.1) whose crossing a member grants while it is at war with the member's liege (PL7's harbouring price, PS §7.7); the service rows the summons writes light under `polityObligationsEnabled`.
- **(f) THE CROWN'S PROTECTION IS FELT — AND RECORDED AS A DEED (PL §4.7 F.4).** THE OCCASION is PL's: a member answers its liege's summons `answered` WITH ITS RIDERS (their line sent, (e)) while the crown's own read is `staunch`+ — the member's roads are then the net's to cover, need (i), first in the net's order; below `staunch` no occasion opens ("a crown that cannot hold the kingdom as one net promises no cover", PL §4.7 F.4). Whether the net actually RODE is a fact PR KEEPS and exports — THE BANNER'S SEASON LEDGER: the member's `weeksCovered` pair (§2.7), the weeks of the season in which a rider of any power OTHER than the member's own (the crown's, a fellow member's) stood placed on at least one of its road halves, counted by the stage from each week's `lived` rows (one placement, R30) and replaced at the season's turn (`seasonStartTick = tick − (tick mod INTERVAL_WEEKS.one_season)`, PL's boundary), read through `bannerCoverOf(worldState, memberId, tick) → { weeks, seasonStartTick, swept }` (§2.6; `swept` the own-need count PL's sweep reads). PL writes ONE row on the member's OWN edge to its immediate liege, whoever's summons it answered (PL §4.1 (i)) — `protection_kept` when `weeks > 0`, `protection_failed` when 0 — at the SEASON'S END (its last tick, after PR's stage has counted that week: §4.1's clock) or at the member's riders' return home if that comes first (the tally so far); an occasion opened in a season's last month is judged on the NEXT season's tally (PL F.4's late-occasion clause); never from the one-week `lived` rows (EXECUTED `cure/D-PR-r5-probe/season-r5.out`: a net that rode weeks 1–10 of a season and then lapsed reads ten weeks and `protection_kept` at the season's end; a stale pair of another season reads 0) (PL's writer, PL's OBLIGATION family; "Came to its aid" is never a number: the PLACEMENT is the deed and the count never enters). PR requests no fealty row and mints none. A BODY sees ONE banner: its believed coverage composes the kingdom's riders by what it has heard (§8.3: `believedWatch` is banner-blind for a body) — deterrence by belief, never by ownership (THE BODIES §5.3 cites it).
- **(g) CIVIL WAR, FEUD AND BREAK — THE GAP CLOSED (PL §4.7 F.5).** A patrol FOLLOWS ITS HOME: its home's lord decides the side, and the banners re-derive at the next read ((a): nothing stored to unwind). PL's three cases (PL §4.7 F.5): a BREAK re-points the breaker's link to `rival`, so the walk no longer reaches its old liege — its patrols follow their home and THE CROWN'S OWN RIDERS in its land are a FOREIGN UNIT: PS's passage rules apply to them as to any army, trespass by hostility included; a FEUD leaves every link sworn and the stack above unchanged, but the two members' patrols lose recognition by their pair's own war state (§10.1 (a), (f): a believed `hostile` makes a meeting `patrol_engages`) and share no eyes; a POLITY WAR keeps every link sworn in storage, but while `swornLinkSuspended` holds the STACK IS CUT at the suspended link — a patrol follows its home's side, and the crown's riders in a claimant-side member's land are a foreign unit. PR's net composition reads the stack THROUGH the predicate and joins PL §5.3's GUARDED set ("the walker reds a reader of the vassal type that does not"); lent riders across a cut or a feud go home. THE FIRST-VISIBLE SIGN (requirement 16): riders TURNED BACK at a border they rode freely last season — where the right has lapsed the next placement asks PS's ladder (§10.1 (c)), and a refusal is the receipt: "Merewick's riders were turned back at the Kell ford, where they rode freely last season."
- **(h) COUNTERFORCES (PL §4.7 F.6).** Riders lent to the net leave the home THINNER — they are taken from its round in reverse priority, so its lowest halves go unridden (§4.1 step 4's rule) — PR's half. PL's halves (PL §4.7 F.6): (a) THE LENDER'S CREDIT — a member whose riders the net placed on a fellow member's road halves in a year writes ONE `service_answered` row that year on its edge to its liege (episode `riders_lent:<rootId>:<yearStartTick>`; PL's row — a row a year, so the 24-row record keeps its oath rows); (b) THE SWEPT LORD'S GRUDGE — the PRECEDENCE FRICTION, ONE `riders_quartered` ROW on the lord's own sworn edge carrying ONE resentment patch, PL's own row and never weave wear: THE SWEEP read at the season's end from the season tally where no cover occasion stood, THE RIDE PAST once per call under `unbroken` ((b) above) (§7.6; PL §4.7 F.6 (b)); (c) THE PEACETIME CALL — a lend in a season in which neither the root nor the lender is at war wears as `riders_lent`, one typed decaying incident per lender per season on its sworn edge (resentment, hence grip: "a crown that calls riders from every member in peace pays in resentment across the chain"), while in war the lend is service and wears nothing; (d) the per-stretch cap stops stacking, and feuding members share no eyes (§10.1 (b)). PR writes none of these: PL's writer reads PR's SEASON TALLY (`bannerCoverOf`, §2.6) and the call's own answers — never the one-week `lived` rows.
- **(i) THE SURFACE (PL §4.7 F.7).** One felt sentence: "The crown's riders keep the Tolle hills since Kell's riders went to the war." The word is never "the crown's wardens": `warden` is CH's ARCHETYPE and the public-security OFFICE that L-BD 41 names — a person who LEADS riders and knows the ways (§5.5, §10.5) — and a landed role label besides (R-8); on one card beside "Ilsa, a warden, rides with Kell's watch" the word would name two things, so the prose keeps PR's "the crown's riders". On scrutiny the card shows the home and the stack as the chain, the hold's band and the need that placed the rider ("Ashford's riders on Kell's half of the ford road, by the crown's banner: Kell's riders are with the levy"); the machinery (the share, the floor, the order of needs) is never pushed. The map draws the banner's colour when the host lights patrols: the window's row carries the stack's `rootId` (§13; H9/H23 — never H16).

### 10.4 The border hand and the escorts (items 4, 12(g); with PS)
- **PS's `granted_under_watch` escort IS a detachment of the holder's riders** (aim `escort`), drawn from the holder's fielded count for the weeks it walks — the riders the escort takes are off the holder's round (the cost). PS §2.3 prices the escort as "subtracted from B's home defence for the weeks it walks"; with PR lit the riders ARE the escort, so the subtraction is from the round, not the walls — PS aligns (a seam row; dark PR keeps PS's home-defence arm, OD-PR-29).
- **CARAVAN AND COLUMN ESCORTS (WY, POP).** A funded court escorts its own caravans and its refugee columns through its own land when a believed threat stands on their route: `escortedOn(worldState, mover, roadKey, tick)` → the escort's band; WY-6's `caravan_seized` odds fall with it and POP's column road deaths fall with it (each consumer's commit; one read) — its two readers recorded by fold §3.1 INBOX rows for WY and POP; PR KEEPS the field, its readers dark until those rows land. Aggregate goods need no escort row: §6.3's presence lean is their escort.
- **A PATROL CROSSING A FOREIGN BORDER** is PS's unit (§10.1(c)); a `barred` answer's contest is PS's, and with PR lit the holder's blocking force against a `quiet` unit may be its riders — PS's card, PS's outcomes (a border hearing, not a screen; PR hosts only the screen's card).

### 10.5 Champions and sites (item 8)
- **A WARDEN LEADS** (CH): a `wildland` paragon of champion polarity whose weekly choice places it with a town's riders (CH §4.4's weights gain a "ride with the watch" row in CH's commit) lifts the quality of the town's riders — ONE bounded MULTIPLIER, CH's `paragonLean(worldState, settlementId, 'wildland', 'patrol_rider_quality')` — the seam word of CH's closed `PARAGON_LEAN_SEAMS` with its declared deviation `d` 0.10 (CH §2.2: "`patrol_rider_quality` 0.10 (PR §10.5's warden riding with a town's riders, applied as `quality × mult`)"; "`→ { mult, reason, wnpcIds } | null`", read from the paragon's `exertion.outcome` only) — applied as `quality × mult`, a factor above one reading toward BETTER riders (an exerting champion's `1 + d`) and below one toward worse (a villain's `1 − d`), PR's own clamp the quality's [0, 1] bound, never a band step and never an additive shift, the receipt naming it ("Ilsa, a warden, rides with Kell's watch"); where a person of the warden archetype HOLDS the town's public-security office (the paragon `securityOfficeOf(worldState, courtId)` returns, then CH's `isWardenParagon`; or an office-holder's roster role `warden`), the town knows the hidden ways of its land (the office's knowledge in BD's one known set `knownTrailsOf`, §5.5; L-BD 41) — a warden merely RETAINED by the court and holding no office adds no way — and a warden may LEAD a hunt (§5.5; L-BD 40); a MAGISTRATE's order (CH's `order` domain) raises the town's will — through CH's seam word `patrol_will` (`PARAGON_LEAN_SEAMS`, its `{ mult }`: a paragon of the `order` calling whose exertion at that town is `exerted`), read as `min(1, WILL_SHARE × mult)` in the WEEKS the exertion stands, never a band step and never "for a season" (a lean on §3.1's WILL, its reason named).
- **A VILLAIN'S NETWORK EVADES OR BUYS THE RIDERS:** its runners meet the one notice law (§8.1 — fame read at the riders' town is their only cover; no cover-identity term exists) and an unpaid watch's `BRIBE_KEEP` (§6.4) applies to them as to goods; exposure is the corruption web's (`applyForeignExposureBlowback`, `covert_hire_exposed`).
- **THE KEEP AND THE RIDERS — two bodies of men, one cap** (ST §7.4's row; OD-PR-41, which corrects the brief's item 3, "the fort's road-danger-down effect IS its patrols"): a held `fortification` puts its attached halves FIRST on its HOLDER's round (§2.2 — a refinement that stays), and the riders who walk them are the holder town's OWN, drawn from its one envelope (§3). The keep's GARRISON is the SITE's own complement — paid through the holder by the site's funding band, drawn from no town's manpower pool and never from its riders; it neither marches nor is drawn (ST §4.1). So the keep's road rows (ST §3.3: presence suppression 0.20, raider counterforce +0.15, the `monsterRaiderGate` ×0.8 · corridor ×0.85 · infiltration ×0.9 rows) STAND while the keep is held and funded, lit or dark, and the riders' rows are the riders' own: two DISTINCT feeders of ONE capped presence suppression (`min(0.25, σ_site + σ_patrol)`, MN §3.3) and ONE counterforce composition (ST's slot, one ±0.15 clamp) — different men, so no double count; the cap is the runaway guard. When the levy takes the riders, the riders' subtraction and deviation go with them (PR3, §9.1(e)) and the keep's rows stay: "the hill road beyond the keep's reach has gone to the brigands" (§18, sentence 9). PR mints no gate row (PR5) and keeps no keep number. A held `watchtower` extends the riders' NOTICE (§8.1's `tower`) and witnesses for them (ST17); DENS are what riders clear (ST's `site_cleared`, §5.3). **SITES CONSIDERED** (ST §7.4's coverage row for PR, "BY BOTH"): the round reads the fort's attachment, the slow half shares ST's suppressor and cap, the clearing is ST's card, the tower is ST's witness — PR keeps no site number and mints no site record.

## §11 THE DM'S HAND AND THE HEARINGS

### 11.1 `PATROL_ORDER` — one realm verb (≤ 4 dials; a realm-manifest row; approval-routed; the edits-delta overlay; premium; the AI proposes it typed)
| Dial | Values | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `settlement` | target: a settlement (the proposal's anchor `targetSaveId`; the actor convention) | whose riders |
| `place` | target: a road half — the stretch key `stretch:<a\|b>` cut at the digest gate, the holder the order's settlement (§2.2) — a site or a BODY (`subject` dial; a body `{ kind: 'body', id }`), or `round` | where the order points; `clear` on a body is the DM's hunt (§5.5) — the quarry rule still binds (a `permitted` conclave is refused, `patrol_quarry_let_be`); the CROWN's order to sweep (THE BODIES §10.2 (b): "the crown may ORDER a sweep through PL's precedence … and PR's `PATROL_ORDER` by the crown") is this verb with the crown's own settlement, its riders reaching a member's body by the banner's right where that member believes the hold strong (PS's read, §10.3 (d)), on the ladder where it believes it weak |
| `priority` | `first · avoid · clear · none` | `first` puts the place at the head of the round (a detachment if a threat is there) — on `round`, every rider the envelope allows rides (`fielded = max`); `avoid` keeps the riders off it (a DM who wants a road unwatched) — on `round`, the count holds at the minimum; `clear` sends a detachment at the place's den or band now; `none` clears the order (§3.2's "lift to `max` or hold at the minimum" is this dial; a standing `first` on `round` held a season is §7.5's second trigger) |
| `wages` | `derived · unfunded · meagre · funded · lavish` | the PAY gate's CEILING (§3.1: the effective band is the lower of this and the purse's band) — the DM moves the input, never the count |
Host: the entry object lives in `patrols/patrolVerbs.js`; `realmManifest.js` (474) spreads it (+≤2), `realmVerbExecution.js` (663) dispatches to `patrols/patrolVerbApply.js` (+≤3) — the leaf-first idiom ST-0 measured. `realmVetoProse` reasons `patrol_gate_dark` · `patrol_place_unknown` · `patrol_none_fielded` · `patrol_quarry_let_be` (a `clear` on a body the court lets be — §5.5's quarry rule; FREE, `cure/D-PR-r4-probe/tokens-r4b.out`). The brief named the verb `PATROL_ORDER: settlement · stretch/gate · priority`; the `wages` dial carries item 9's "the DM moves its inputs: wages" on the same card (ST's `SITE_STATUS` precedent of a funding dial); security and war are moved by the DM's existing hands (Edit Mode's institutions; the war verbs).

### 11.2 THE HEARINGS (requirement 24 — the engagement-registry rows THE PATROLS owns; each registered with SP-9 in the wave that builds it)
| Kind | Owner (wave) | Climax tick | Verdict vocabulary | Class | WHERE (`ENGAGEMENT_WHERE_KINDS`) | Default verdict | Parties |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `patrol_engages` | PR (PR-2; the screen at PR-3) | the week a patrol or detachment comes to blows with a hostile force on a road that no other kind hears: an army marching on the riders' own town or crossing its land (the screen), a BODY (THE BODIES) its court pursues — a `hunted` gang, an `outlawed` conclave, a beast — met on the road, at a known trail's junction or in the country (THE HUNT's catch, §5.5), or any body that seeks out riders it outmatches (the owner's word 14; BD14), another power's riders believed hostile (a feud, a stale belief, a broken banner — §10.3 (g)) | `driven_off · screened · withdrew · cut_down` — `driven_off` (the foe beaten off; ST's `site_taken` word in the same sense), `screened` (held an army one week), `withdrew` (fell back with the word; no delay), `cut_down` (destroyed; against an army the week was still bought) | CLIMAX | `road (a, b)` — the stretch (a catch on a way beside it is heard here too, the card naming it "in the forest beside the hill road", BD §9.1's idiom; at a junction the map has NAMED the card's key is `junction:<id>` under `road (a, b)`, never a sixth WHERE kind — the fold registers the key); `country (a)` for a hunt's catch off the roads (the fold's WHERE companion takes the cell) | the contest: the force's band against the foe's on `SIEGE_VERDICT_BANDS` — a body's force is BD's `bodyStrength` — one keyed draw (stream `patrol_engages:<detachmentId>:<tick>`); for a screen, `screened` vs `withdrew` by the court's fork (a held site on the stretch, a mustering levy → `screened`), then `cut_down` by the band ratio | `{ kind: 'settlement', id }` (the riders' town — the proposal's anchor `targetSaveId`) upon the foe: `{ kind: 'settlement', id }` for an army or riders; `{ kind: 'corps', id: unitKey }` for a retained company on the road (RN keys it `u:<unitKey>`); `{ kind: 'body', id }` for a body, on the card's `subject` dial (its `bodyKind` a field; RN keys a gang `g:<bodyId>`) — the actor convention's SIX kinds `settlement · person · party · menace · corps · body`; never `{ kind: 'menace' }` (a menace is MN's `menace_met`) and never a site (a site is a WHERE and a subject, never an actor) |
| `patrol_joins` | PR (PR-4) | the week a town asks an ally to join a detachment (§10.1(d)) | `rides · will_see · refused` | CLIMAX | `road (a, b)` — the shared stretch, else `country (ally)` | the ally's fork: the landed relationship pair state (label, trust, resentment) and its own believed danger (§10.1(d)) | the ally upon the asker |

**Read here, owned elsewhere (never a second card for one fact):** PS's four kinds (`passage_at_the_border` for a patrol crossing, and its ENVOY ARM for an envoy the riders stop; `armed_incursion` for an enemy in a third town's land, whose home-defence battle stays PS's while the riders' screen is PR's card); ST's `site_cleared` (a detachment clearing a den); MN's `menace_met` (a detachment grouped to a menace's band — MN's producer row; `army_beaten` reads "an army or a detachment"); ES's `spy_at_target` at WHERE `road` (`caught · uncaught`); WY-6's `caravan_seized`; PT's `party_gate` at WHERE `road` and PT's road WATCH kind `party_ambush` (a `party_warrant` stands at the court's town — PT's row) (ruled, OD-PR-14); PL's `call_in_answer` for the crown's call on a member's riders; THE BODIES' `bodies_meet` (two bodies met through this volume's ONE encounter law, §8.1 — BD's card), `body_stance` (the court's stance that decides whom the riders hunt, §5.5 — BD's card) and the hunt MISSION card of L-BD 40 (`hunt_taken`, its owning volume's — PR's detachment is only its unit). NEVER read here: `envoy_intercepted` · `envoy_held` — the army interception's; a patrol never holds an envoy (ES-2b, OPENED by delegation, admits only the shapes as written — §8.2's paragon row among them, an envoy not). **Not hearings:** the round, the cadence, grouping and dispersal, looks and their reports, the presence and counter leans, the witness read — receipts only (they decide nothing between two parties). The brief's `patrol_engages` verdicts `repelled · withdrew · destroyed` and the screen's `screened · withdrew · destroyed` are not used: `repelled` is ST's `site_cleared` verdict in the OPPOSITE sense (the clearer repelled) on the same docket, and `destroyed` is a landed literal in 27 files (institution and site events); ONE vocabulary covers both acts.

**Requirement 24's three choices on every card:** LEAVE it (the default resolves at the next advance), TILT it (`slight · heavy`, a draw bias ±0.05 / ±0.15 on the contest — MN's idiom), or DECIDE it (the DM's word is the verdict; never "decree"). Only at a ONE-WEEK advance is a kind minted and paused; MONTH, SEASON and YEAR advances resolve every week on the default and leave each week's receipt; nothing queues. The proposals queue's anchor `targetSaveId` is always a real settlement (the riders' town, the ally); the counterparty rides the `subject` dial (the actor convention). No PR kind is a party kind (PR19); requirement 24's party default never arises here. **The card** (the Evidence Law): "Kell's riders have met Merewick's van at the ford. Left alone, they hold it a week." · "Ashford asks Tolle's riders to ride with it against the band in the hills. Left alone, Tolle will see." The registry count rises by TWO kind names in two rows (the fold restates it; there is no `patrol_order` kind — the crown's call on a member's riders is PL's summons, §10.3 (e)).

| Flag | What it lights | Host flags it requires | Presets it may light in | Lit-mutant pins (each reds when its arm is planted dark) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `patrolsEnabled` | the envelope, the round, the cadence clock, the stage, the placement digest, detachments, looks, reports, the witness read (PS8 → PR's read), the encounter law (its body arms included), the believed-watch read, THE HUNT (§5.5), THE BANNER (§10.3: the stack, the right, the net, the cover read), `PATROL_ORDER`, the two hearings, the window's patrol rows (§13) | `hearingWeekEnabled` for the pause (dark ⇒ every card on its default in its own tick); the rumor road (infoMode `full`) for reports that TRAVEL (else a news line / the omniscient arm); `warLayerEnabled` for armies and the screen; `polityHoldEnabled` for the crown's riders and THE BANNER's right and net (the hold's band is the gate, §10.3 (d)); `bodiesEnabled` (THE BODIES) for a body as a traveller, a foe and a quarry; `hiddenWaysEnabled` for trail coverage, the known-way round term and a warden's ways; `bodyEffectsEnabled` (THE BODIES' second flag) for a LED hunt — BD's `hunt_taken` card and its detachment (BD §9.11); `routeLifecycleEnabled` only for a way's RUNG to move (dark: the candidates alone at the hidden price, BD §5.2); `habitConditioningEnabled` for the round's habit term (§2.2; dark: the in-horizon incident count); `polityObligationsEnabled` for the riders' line on PL-5's summons (relief, protection and service rows); `npcConsequencesEnabled` (persons) for paragon encounters (OD-FC-39's lighting unit); `errandSpineEnabled` / `espionageEnabled` for envoy and spy encounters; `worldSitesEnabled` for sites; `passageEnabled` for crossings | every preset where its arms' host flags are lit — the round and the reports are aspatial-safe (graph hops); the danger arm canonized only | (1) a planted patrol with the flag dark ⇒ the dormancy golden reds; (2) a planted `Math.random` / unkeyed draw ⇒ the determinism pin (two runs) reds; (3) a planted second presence feeder or a planted write to the embattlement level ⇒ the one-danger pin reds; (4) a planted stressor gate row from `patrols/` ⇒ the no-gate-row walker reds; (5) a planted hearing resolved in its own tick at a one-week advance ⇒ the docket walker reds; (6) a planted count above the tier ceiling ⇒ the envelope pin reds; (7) a planted consumer deriving its own placement ⇒ the one-placement pin (R30) reds; (8) a planted presence hash keyed by the week ⇒ the by-address pin (R31) reds; (9) a planted pool read from `facets.manpower` ⇒ the within-tier population pin (R2) reds; (10) a planted allocation that puts one rider on two halves in one week, or a half present off its word's period ⇒ the one-place pin (R31) reds; (11) a planted coverage on a trail the power does not know ⇒ the trail-belief pin (R43) reds; (12) a planted pursuit of a `permitted`, `endowed`, `tolerated`, `employed`, `harboured` or `curbed` body ⇒ the quarry pin (R45) reds; (13) a planted by-right crossing where the holder believes the hold weak, across a suspended link or between feuding members, a planted lent rider on a half whose holder answers `tolerance`, or a planted net rider displacing a home's own ⇒ the banner pins (R39–R41) red; (14) a planted stored loyalty stack or stored hunt clock ⇒ the derived-state walker reds; (15) a planted change to a leader's record on a `cut_down` ⇒ the no-named-fate pin (R46) reds; (16) a planted member with two or more riders on the round lending none under the crown's `staunch`+ read, or a lender left below `NET_HOME_MIN`, or a sub-vassal's lend computed at the crown's own share ⇒ the lend pin (R51) reds; (17) a planted protection outcome read from the one-week `lived` rows, or a season tally summed across a season's turn ⇒ the season-ledger pin (R50) reds; (18) a planted patrol move on a hidden way outside the riders' franchise (a round, a screen, an escort or a look on a way; a detachment above `modest`; a way the town does not know or beyond its `upTo`; a border crossed by a way) or a way ridden at any price but the landed hidden grade's ⇒ the franchise pin (R52) reds; (19) a planted `patrol_clash` deed inside a LED hunt, or a led hunt's success minted by PR ⇒ the one-deed pin (R53) reds; (20) a planted trail coverage that reads the stored `watched` mark in place of the week's placement, or a known way's coverage from any set but `knownTrailsOf` ⇒ the trail-belief pin (R43) reds |
| `patrolEffectsEnabled` | §6.1 the presence subtraction · §6.2 the counter term · §6.3 the fast roll · §6.4 the smuggling watch · §6.6 the draw · §7.5 the harassment input | `patrolsEnabled`; the canon marker (§6.1); THE SHARED PRESENCE COMPOSER (§6.1 — it lights when any registered feeder's own flag is lit, so PR's subtraction requires `patrolEffectsEnabled` alone, never `menacesEnabled`); `commodityFlowEnabled` (§6.3–6.4); `warLayerEnabled` (§6.6); `commonsVoiceEnabled` (§7.5) | as `patrolsEnabled` | (1) dark ⇒ every host's lean returns its neutral (`watch01` absent, `patrolDraw` absent, the term 0) and the dormancy golden is byte-identical; (2) a planted `watch01` that changes `banditryLoss`'s LOSS SIZE ⇒ the roll-not-size pin reds; (3) a planted subtraction on the walls term ⇒ the latch pin reds; (4) a planted counter term outside ST's one clamp, or a planted `menaceCounterTerms` row from `patrols/` ⇒ the clamp / one-entry pin reds; (5) a planted draw on the per-tick `offensive` or `homeDefense`, or a feasibility gate reading the undrawn `fromCap` while riders are kept ⇒ the deploy-draw pin (R9) reds; (6) a planted route-wide MAX, or a watch on a sea hop ⇒ the per-hop pin (R6, R25) reds; (7) a planted harassment input with no fork and no standing order, or with no rider added, or before `forkSince` (or the order's `setTick`) has held one season ⇒ the harassment pin (R35, R37) reds; (8) a planted `drawShare01` that is `NaN`, or below 1 where nothing was drawn, or a stamp whose `points` exceed the offensive drawn ⇒ the draw-guard pin (R38) reds; (9) a planted deployment whose court kept riders and carries no stamp ⇒ the kept-stamp pin (R38 (a)) reds |

**The omniscient default's arm, named:** in `realistic_regional` (omniscient; no rumor road; the war layer dark) the riders still ride and their effects run where lit — reports are chronicle lines (the town knows the truth in its land), no false report exists, and no army is screened. **The host lighting-claim note:** "lit in a preset" is trusted only after the BIRTH-DOOR CHECK (a born campaign carries the default preset's name but none of its switches). **The same-seed shift:** lighting `patrolEffectsEnabled` in any preset is a DECLARED same-seed shift (every embattled world's danger, banditry and smuggling move, and every war in which a court keeps riders marches a smaller army, which its opener judges feasible or not as the smaller army — some targets drop out of reach), signed at its lighting unit; lighting `patrolsEnabled` alone moves only PS's witness read (from always-witnessed to watched) — itself a declared shift where `passageEnabled` is lit.

**THE LIGHTING PRECONDITION** (a cross-program row; the map program's half): `patrolEffectsEnabled` lights in a world only where the map's patrol lens is ABSENT or has ADOPTED the host's patrol rows and its new legend (§13) — never where the lens still draws its own sway cadence under "patrols change nothing in the simulation" while the host's riders change danger; the lighting unit's certification row checks it, and the fold's map-side inbox carries the ask.

## §13 THE WINDOW, NOT THE DOOR (the owner's decision in the host document; item 18)

- **WHILE PR IS DARK** the map's patrols are its own picture — its sway-derived cadence (V13 §3.9 (14)), its legend true ("patrols change nothing in the simulation"); the host is asked for nothing (the owner's decision in the host document).
- **WHEN PR LIGHTS** the host SENDS its patrol facts OUT THROUGH THE WINDOW, as it sends every fact: the dated acts (a clash, a catch, a screen, a look's report) in the EVENTS window (H9: "committed facts only … each fact with who may see it"), and the week just lived per road half — `{ road: stretchKey, half: holder, power, command, cadence (PATROL_CADENCES), on (the week's PRESENCE as it was), state? (on_round · engaged · escorting), detachment? (band, aim), banner? (the power's stack's `rootId` — derived, so the map may draw the banner's colour, §10.3 (i)) }`, read from the placement digest's `lived` row (§2.7) — as a ledger row of the LENS window beside it (H23: "the ledger rows the host already keeps … each with who may see it"), where the map's patrol layer, which already takes its inputs from the polity window's patrol rows (H13: "the whole input of the sway and of patrols"), can draw it on M-W5's sway-and-patrols step (H25) (H9 carries the dated acts; the host document's own rows for a standing ledger fact are H23 beside H9, and H13 for the polity inputs — the row's name is the map program's). Each fact is at most one advance stale and carries its resolved audience (DM-only by default; a covert power's riders fail closed, the map's own rule). THE HOST'S ASK (the map program decides; no FP text supersedes a map derivation — the fold's §3.1.26): draw PRESENCE AS SENT rather than recompute it from the cadence word — the map's own phase is keyed from the road segment's lineage, the host's from the road and the power, so two clocks would part and a drawn rider would stand beside "Kell's company slipped through unseen". The cadence word means the same on both sides (a period, PR4) and DWELLS (§2.3), so a drawn picture changes with the world, not with each week's re-bucketing. The host asks, too, that the lens's legend change when it draws the host's rows ("the host's riders"; its regime banner states it — the map's honesty rule). A CROSS-PROGRAM NOTE back to the map program, for the fold's §3.1 map-side inbox (h): "draw the host's patrol rows when sent; your legend changes then" (the fold's row).
- **H16 STAYS SHUT, AND OPEN AS THE OWNER LEFT IT.** H16 is the map's INBOUND door ("patrol coverage re-priced through one distance reader, read only by outcome rolls"; "The only door through which patrols or the sway can ever touch the simulation") — the door through which the MAP's coverage might one day touch the host. PR never opens it and never consumes the map's coverage: the host's riders derive from the host's own garrison, wages, security and belief (§3); the map's `patrolOffer` stays an offer no host reader reads (the host document: "No host consumer exists today, and none is asked for"). PR therefore does NOT answer trade-OQ4 ("a host meaning for patrol coverage" — the MAP's coverage, which the owner decided not to ask) and claims nothing on trade-OQ6; `route_wardenship` stays EC-6's stance term (OD-FC-2), a commercial reason between two courts, not a patrol.
- **CADENCE NEVER REACHES THE HOST AS A TIME** from the map; the host sends its own cadence as a FACT (a word) with its own presence, and the map's law ("the host prices any round trip with its own distance reader") is untouched because the host's riders are priced by the host's own reader (`hopWeeks`). Water carries no patrol fact (PR15).
- **THE LIGHTING PRECONDITION** (§12): `patrolEffectsEnabled` lights only where the map's lens is absent or has adopted these rows and the new legend.
